### 2026-09-28c (Australia/Sydney) — an edit made outside the mutation barrier is recorded before the next managed write, not laundered into it
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/knowledge_revision.py` (`external_observations` table, `DriftUnobservable`, `observe_external_drift(trigger=)` records each mint, `_absorb_external_drift` called from `_insert_mutation`), `scripts/cli.py` (`knowledge status` prints `drift:`), new `tests/test_external_drift.py` (12), design §5.2 note, `CLAUDE.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-28-z0-drift-absorbed-at-activation.md`. Branch `z0-drift-at-activation`. Closes 2026-09-24 item 3, with the bound below.
- **Root cause, found while planning Z0 step 5.** Step 2 built `observe_external_drift` and nothing in production called it. `commit_mutation` records the manifest of the WHOLE store, so a hand edit made between two managed writes was absorbed into the second one's revision and labelled MANAGED. Reproduced in a throwaway store before any change: revision 2 MANAGED, its digest covering the hand edit, and drift detection afterwards returning `None` — the evidence overwritten. That is Z0 gate 13's "atomicity is not laundered", violated, and it would have made step 5's `built_from_revision` name the right bytes with the wrong history. It is also why the 2026-09-24 hand edit left `knowledge status` at CLEAN.
- **Fix.** Before a managed operation's first byte, `_insert_mutation` compares the live manifest with the recorded one and, if they differ, mints an `EXTERNAL_OBSERVED` revision first (`trigger="activation"`); the managed write then commits on top of it. Each mint is kept in `external_observations`, because the managed commit that follows overwrites `atomicity`. `knowledge status` reports `drift: UNRECORDED` read-only.
- **Bound, stated rather than hidden.** No mint — and the edit is absorbed as before — while the store is still changing (Obsidian mid-save), while another operation is in flight (its half-written files are not external), on a SUSPECT store (reconcile owns it), or with no baseline. Capture-time detection, Z0 §10.2 item 12 as written, is still step 5/6.
- **Measured.** One manifest pass per activation: 60 ms median over 4,605 files (n=7, warm; one cold run 174 ms — the figure first quoted, and the wrong one to lead with). A simulation that forced absorption before every mutation across the whole suite ran 1215 passed / 0 failed before any code was written.
- **Falsified.** Removing absorption turns `test_a_hand_edit_becomes_its_own_revision_before_the_managed_write` red; dropping the observation row turns that and `test_a_manual_observation_is_recorded` red. The plan also claimed the CLI end-to-end test would redden under an in-process plugin; it cannot reach a subprocess, and its red evidence is its failure on the pre-fix code — recorded as a ruling, not glossed.
- **Verification.** `check.sh` **EXIT=0**, pytest **1232 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed** (1215 + 12 + 5 plan-integrity cases) before the review; **1238 passed / 0 failed** after the fix pass, red-team **27/27**, `public_repo_guard` passed, `git diff --check` clean, live `wiki/*.md` byte-identical across the gate.
- **Final review (fresh reviewer, Opus): no Critical, two Important — both real, both fixed.** (1) Lazy activation minted an operation's OWN direct writes as an external edit when it wrote authoritative files before reaching `safe_write` (`extract`); `ingest` never reached the barrier at all, because `wiki_merge.append_log_event` writes `wiki/log.md` directly. Such operations now activate **eagerly** (`operation(eager=True)`): drift is absorbed before their first byte and their direct writes commit as their own MANAGED revision; an eager operation that changed nothing ends ABORTED. (2) A crashed writer's leftover ACTIVE row disabled absorption until reconcile; `_absorb_external_drift` now runs `detect_orphans` first (DEAD owners only, pid + start time), so a torn crash becomes a loud SUSPECT instead. `knowledge status` no longer promises a mint on a SUSPECT store or while an operation is in flight. New tests went red first where the code did not yet exist; the two eager-mode tests were written after the code and are shown by falsification instead.
- **Wording corrected.** EXTERNAL_OBSERVED means *changed outside the mutation barrier* — an editor, **or a Zurvan writer that bypasses `safe_write`** (§4.1 already defines those as unmanaged). The first version of this entry said "outside Zurvan"; for a standalone direct writer that was false.
- **A production-store isolation violation, caused by this work and repaired.** The first ingest test ran `ingest.py` as a subprocess under `ZURVAN_ROOT` and wrote a row (id 4473) into the LIVE `data/registry.sqlite` `sources` table: `scripts/db.py` pinned that table to the repo while the ledger in the same file followed `ZURVAN_ROOT`. The live wiki was untouched (`config.PROJECT_ROOT` follows `ZURVAN_ROOT`). The row was removed after a backup (`~/.zurvan/forensics/registry.sqlite.bak-2026-09-28-before-row4473`), and `db.py` now resolves the registry from the store root per call (`DB_PATH=None`, the test override still works). Pinned by `test_the_source_registry_follows_zurvan_root`, written as a read so its red run could not contaminate anything. A full suite afterwards left zero test rows in the live registry.
- **Bounds, complete this time.** No mint — the edit is absorbed as before — while the store is still changing, while another operation is live, on a SUSPECT store, with no baseline, or when the edit lands **during** a managed operation (between its activation and its commit).
- **Deferred minors from the review:** the SUSPECT race inside `observe_external_drift` raises plain `LedgerError` (refuses the write, before any byte); the unstable-store test monkeypatches the observer rather than racing a real edit (the real path was probed and holds); `external_observations()` raises raw `OperationalError` on a ledger never migrated.
- **Follow-ups.** (1) Z0 step 5 — snapshot-backed rebuilds, `built_from_revision`, health states — now rests on revisions that record their provenance. (2) The unstable-store bound: an edit landing mid-save is still absorbed.

### 2026-09-28b (Australia/Sydney) — Z0 step 4: the graph is published atomically, and a published store refuses an in-place write
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/sqlite_publish.py` (new: the publication protocol), `scripts/graph_build.py` (collect / write aside / publish), `scripts/graph_schema.py` (`graph_meta`, `read_graph_meta`, `content_digest`), `scripts/rebuild_search_index.py` (promotes through the protocol; sweep moved out, old name kept as an alias), `scripts/measurement_snapshot.py` (`capture_search` refuses a writable index), new `tests/test_sqlite_publish.py` (25) and `tests/test_graph_publication.py` (11 — 10 at first, one added by the review fix pass), `tests/test_snapshot_components.py` (gate 14 flipped from "reproduced" to "enforced"), `tests/test_index_integrity.py` and `tests/test_hybrid_search.py` (named unlock helper), design §4.4 and §10.3, `CLAUDE.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-28-z0-step4-atomic-graph-publication.md` rev 2. Branch `z0-step4-atomic-graph`.
- **Gates 14 and 15 are CLOSED, with a bound.** A published store is mode 0444: an in-place SQLite write raises `attempt to write a readonly database`, a byte write raises `PermissionError`, and `os.replace` onto it still works. **Bound: the owner can chmod it back and root ignores it** — it stops an accidental in-place write, the hazard §4.2 names, and is not a security boundary. Publication refuses any sidecar, any journal_mode but `delete`, any unreadable database and (graph) any `integrity_check` other than `ok`, before the swap. Z0 stays OPEN: `built_from_revision`, snapshot-backed rebuilds and coherence verification are NOT BUILT.
- **The plan was gauntleted before a line was written, by running it, and four premises were wrong.** A corruption test that corrupted nothing (512 bytes landed in unused space; `integrity_check` said ok). Real corruption raises from the PRAGMA rather than returning rows. **Graph builds were not reproducible**: `edge_id` is `uuid4()` per build (`graph_build.py:84`), so the before/after proof would have failed for a reason unrelated to the change — content comparison now excludes it, and deterministic ids are filed. The falsification table claimed an order test catches a missing fsync; a dry run showed it cannot. Ten existing tests that write a published store in place were found by simulating read-only publication, and named in the plan.
- **Ranking-neutral, proven on frozen inputs, not asserted.** A clonefile-frozen corpus built with the old code and the new: graph content digest identical (`b4de9b0c…`, 4,682 nodes / 3,946 edges, reproduced by a second old-code build first), search `chunk_id_set_sha` identical (`cc0fcb51…`, 186,871 chunks). Live eval 72% / 74% before (chunk-set `caf8ba2f41d0`) and after (`387c90eb26b8`) — **not comparable**, the corpus moved; the claim rests on the frozen build.
- **Falsified, all behavioural.** Removing the chmod turns 8 tests red, the self-containment check 5, validate-before-swap 18, the fsyncs 3. The fsync tests prove the OS primitive is called, never that bytes survive a power cut.
- **Measured.** Publication: graph 14-17 ms including `integrity_check`, search 144-625 ms (lower bound — APFS clone copies leave little dirty data). A real SIGKILL just before the swap leaves the published graph byte-identical; the next build sweeps the orphan.
- **Live stores republished** through the new path: `data/search.sqlite` and `data/graph.sqlite` are `-r--r--r--`, no sidecars; `graph_meta` stamped (generation `c79a9e6e…`).
- **Two rulings during execution:** both index builders in `test_hybrid_search` needed the unlock (the plan said one); the publish-cost script chmods its copy writable first.
- **Verification.** `check.sh` **EXIT=0**, pytest **1214 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed** at the gate (floor 1177 + 25 + 10 + 1 index test + 1 net in `test_snapshot_components`: two added, the reproduced-hazard test replaced); **1215** after the fix pass, red-team **27/27**, `public_repo_guard` passed, `git diff --check` clean, live `wiki/*.md` byte-identical across the gate.
- **Final review (fresh reviewer, Opus): no Critical, two Important, both fixed RED→GREEN.** (1) The disk-full test replaced `_write_graph` whole, so no build file ever existed and its cleanup assertion was vacuous — the reviewer showed it passing with the cleanup deleted. It now opens a real write before failing; with cleanup removed it goes red (`graph.sqlite.building-<pid>` and its `-journal` left). (2) The build file appeared only in `_write_graph`, after ~3 s of collecting the live corpus, so a second build started in that window passed the sweep. The path is now claimed before collecting; `test_a_build_still_collecting_already_blocks_a_second` was `DID NOT RAISE` before the fix. Suite after the pass: **1215 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed**. The same review corrected this entry's test arithmetic (it said 11 graph tests when 10 existed). Observed on republish: the live graph shrank 13.2 MB → 4.6 MB — the old DELETE-then-INSERT build left free pages; a fresh build has none.
- **Follow-ups.** (1) Step 5: `built_from_revision` / `built_from_snapshot`, snapshot-backed rebuilds. (2) `edge_id` is random per build — any consumer caching edge ids is wrong across generations. (3) The build sweep decides liveness by bare pid (reuse race). (4) Graph readers open read-write and would create an empty file at a missing canonical path. (5) Hand edits invisible between reconciles — must close before Z0.

### 2026-09-28 (Australia/Sydney) — writes outside an operation are refused; the 09-23 six-revision split traced to its source
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/knowledge_revision.py` (`KnowledgeOperationRequired`, `require_active_operation`, `allow_implicit_for_migration`, `mutations.implicit_reason` + migration, `resolve_implicit(reason=)`), `scripts/memory.py` (writers refuse up front; `InvalidProjectError`), `scripts/cli.py` (`_split_global_project`; dependency message), `scripts/mcp_tools.py`, `scripts/ingest.py` and `scripts/extract.py` (`__main__` boundaries), new `tests/test_fail_closed_writes.py` (35), six existing test files moved onto explicit operations, Z0 design §3.2 and amendments spec §9 (amendment 7), `CLAUDE.md`. Branch `z0-fail-closed-writes`. Reverses the 2026-09-19b decision that direct library writes stay "implicit and inventoried".
- **What the ledger showed.** Six unresolved implicit mutations, revisions 73-78, one process (pid 22012, 2026-09-23 14:47:18), alternating `add_decision`/`add_note` with `append_to_log`: three logical writes minted six revisions, so a snapshot between a page and its log line would have named a decision the log does not mention. The same half-operation 19b fixed for MCP, from a caller neither the CLI nor MCP boundary covers. Evidence frozen before anything changed: `~/.zurvan/forensics/2026-09-28-implicit-mutations-pid22012*.json` (sha256 recorded in the session), plus a registry backup taken before resolution.
- **Root cause, from the writer's own transcript, not inferred.** The writer was an Antigravity (Gemini CLI) agent in a client repository. (1) `PYTHONPATH=. python3 scripts/cli.py --help` died with `ModuleNotFoundError: yaml` — Homebrew `python3` is not the interpreter `CLAUDE.md` assumes. (2) `uv run` worked, but it needed `decision add --project` and that flag never arrived (the 19b-filed `cli.py:42` defect). (3) It read `memory.py` and ran `uv run python -c "from scripts.memory import add_decision, add_note; ..."` — the process start time matches the ledger's `owner_identity` exactly. (4) `resolve_project("ClientX")`-shaped input answered `unknown` silently (`is_safe_slug` is lowercase-only). (5) It then hand-edited the pages' `project:` field, and edited two of them again on 24 and 26 September; the ledger reported CLEAN throughout, confirming 2026-09-24 item 3 on live data.
- **The fix is fail-closed at the choke point, with the library guard in front of it.** `plan_write` — the one place every `safe_write` passes — raises `KnowledgeOperationRequired` for an authoritative write with no operation on its store, before the bytes and before a ledger row. `memory`'s four public writers refuse first, naming themselves and the way out (CLI or `with kr.operation(...)`). Implicit mode survives only inside `allow_implicit_for_migration(reason)`: no boolean form, reason mandatory, recorded in the new `implicit_reason` column (historical rows keep NULL; migration re-reads `PRAGMA table_info` inside its transaction, as 19b's did).
- **Companion fixes, each a link in the chain above.** `--project` switches workspace only BEFORE the subcommand, so `decision add --project <slug>` reaches the page (the `agent prime` hook was unaffected — it already uses `--for-project`). An explicit malformed project raises `InvalidProjectError` with a suggestion, and is **not** lowercased on the caller's behalf: the subject is a publication control. `cli.py` under an interpreter without PyYAML names `scripts/zurvan` instead of a traceback. The MCP decision tool returns `Error: ...` for a malformed project. `ingest.py` and `extract.py` open their own operation when run as `__main__` (the e2e scripts run them that way).
- **Falsified, three ways.** Re-opening implicit writes globally turns all 8 refusal tests red. Removing `memory`'s own guard leaves the backstop alone — and exposed that `add_question` was not reaching the backstop in the test at all (its router still pointed at the live repo, so the path guard refused first); the fixture was fixed and the backstop then held every library case. Restoring the old anywhere-pop `--project` turns 3 CLI tests red. The live question files were checked unchanged after that falsification.
- **Two gate findings, neither from this change, both fixed.** (1) The red-team gate was **26/27 on arrival**: the 2026-09-24 entry (commit `807045a`, local, never pushed) carried a private project name in both `AGENTS.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`. Redacted in place to `otherproj` — the one kind of edit to a committed record this project makes, per the 19b precedent — and recorded here so it is auditable. (2) A client name was going to enter tracked code, tests and specs a dozen times as the incident's example; replaced with `ClientX`/`clientx`. The real names live only in the private forensic bundle.
- **Verification.** `check.sh` **all gates, EXIT=0**, pytest inside it **1172 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed** (floor: 1137 at `02648c7`, identical code), red-team **27/27**, `public_repo_guard` passed, `git diff --check` clean. A pre-change baseline was attempted and is **invalid**: it was still running when the edits began, and its one failure was a child process loading the new code. The independent `wiki/*.md` hash comparison reported a change during the gate: `wiki/log.md` and `open-questions.private.md`, written by pid 16899 at 08:15:18 — a `question add` from another project's session, one operation, one revision (195), no test-session token. External, correctly classified by the session guard, and itself running on the new code. The gate's own index rebuild moved the corpus to 186,776 chunks (`chunk-set dc7b46531b6c`); eval Top-1 72% / Top-3 74%. **Not comparable** to 09-19's 76% (different chunk-set), and no retrieval code changed.
- **Live ledger.** The six rows are resolved, not erased: still `implicit=1`, `resolution_actor=Raouf`, reason naming the closed path and the forensic file. `zurvan knowledge callers`: **No unresolved implicit callers.** CLEAN, MANAGED.
- **Follow-ups.** (1) Hand edits remain invisible to the ledger between reconciles (2026-09-24 item 3) — the EXTERNAL_OBSERVED path, its own change. (2) The remaining 2026-09-24 items: `project register` validation and `unregister`; `claim add` records no subject. (3) Z0 step 4 (atomic graph publication). (4) Any external script that imported `scripts.memory` now fails loudly — intended; the message names the fix.

### 2026-09-24 (Australia/Sydney) — issues found writing back from gmail-mcp, left OPEN for another agent
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** no code changed. This entry records five defects hit while writing the gmail-mcp session back to Zurvan from outside the Zurvan repo, so the next agent can fix them. Written from `~/Desktop/Raouf/gmail` with the registry's `current` set to `otherproj`.
- **1. A non-Zurvan repo cannot be named as a subject.** `decision add` run from `~/Desktop/Raouf/gmail` stamped `project: "unknown"` on `wiki/decisions/gmail-mcp-an-owners-saved-allow-is-final-modifiers.md`. Two older gmail decisions carry the same `unknown` stamp, and one says `gmail`, so earlier sessions hit this too. `--project gmail` answers `Project 'gmail' not registered`. Registering it makes things worse (item 2). No path exists from the CLI to stamp a subject for a repo that is not itself a Zurvan workspace, and gmail-mcp is exactly that case.
- **2. `project register` accepts a path it will then refuse to use.** `project register --name gmail --path ~/Desktop/Raouf/gmail` printed `Registered project 'gmail'`, `project list` then showed it `❌ (missing/invalid)`, and every `claim add --project gmail` failed with `ValueError: Path is not a valid Zurvan project`. Register should validate first and refuse. There is also no `project unregister` or `remove`: the entry was deleted by editing `~/.zurvan/projects.json` by hand, with a backup left at `~/.zurvan/projects.json.bak-2026-09-24`.
- **3. A hand edit of a wiki page is invisible to the ledger.** To work around item 1, the decision page's `project: "unknown"` was changed to `project: "gmail"` with `sed`, outside any mutation. Afterwards `knowledge status` reported `CLEAN revision 105` and `knowledge doctor` reported `no orphans`. Either drift detection does not cover page content between reconciles, or it covers it and something else explains the CLEAN. Confirm which before trusting CLEAN after out-of-band edits. The page and its ledger row may now disagree about the subject, which 2026-09-19b set out to make unrepresentable.
- **4. `claim add` records no subject at all.** Claims carry `type`, `confidence`, `source` and `tags`, and no `project:` field, while decisions carry one. The four gmail claims written today (`claim-7e1c87a1`, `claim-11a71f17`, `claim-2fe93d05`, `claim-bd61a80d`) are findable only through the `gmail-mcp` tag. A project-scoped digest keyed on `project:` will not see them.
- **5. The write-back hook's advice cannot be followed from a non-workspace repo.** The PostToolUse reminder says to check the `project:` field `decision add` wrote, because it stamps the active workspace. From gmail, checking finds `unknown`, and items 1 and 2 leave no supported way to correct it.
- **Suggested order:** item 2 first (validate on register and add `unregister`), then give `decision add` and `claim add` a subject that does not require a Zurvan workspace (a lightweight `subject` registry, or accept an unregistered `--project` string and record it as unverified), then item 3.
- **Verification of this report:** every quoted message was copied from terminal output in this session. `knowledge status` and `knowledge doctor` were run after the hand edit. Nothing here was re-tested after writing, and none of it is fixed.

### 2026-09-19c (Australia/Sydney) — Z0 step 3: immutable snapshot bytes, and the capture-cost estimate was wrong by 5x
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/immutable_bytes.py` (new), `scripts/measurement_snapshot.py` (components, read lock, build-then-rename publication), four new test files (`test_immutable_bytes.py`, `test_wiki_immutability_matrix.py`, `test_snapshot_components.py`, `test_snapshot_backend_equivalence.py`), `tests/test_measurement_snapshot.py` (append only), design §4.1/§10.4/§12 corrected. 8 commits on `z0-step3-immutable-bytes`. Implements §9.2 step 3.
- **This does NOT close Z0, and closes exactly ONE of the twenty gate items.** Gate 16 (wiki immutability matrix) is closed. **Gate 14 is OPEN**: the tests reproduce the hardlink hazard and refuse a capture taken over a journal, but nothing makes a direct in-place write to the published inode fail. Demonstrating a failure mode is not preventing it. Graph atomic publication NOT BUILT; coherence verification NOT BUILT; `built_from_revision` NOT BUILT; authoritative snapshot consumption NOT BUILT. **Z0 OPEN.**
- **The measurement is worse than the estimate, which is why §10.4 says an estimate is not a result.** A full three-component capture on the live store costs **5,904 ms** against the design's `~1.1 s`, with peak RSS delta **8.34 MB** against the `1.20 MB` §12.1 asked about, freezing 884.1 MB. The estimate was not wrong about bytes — freezing them costs ~120 ms. It counted the cost of FREEZING bytes and omitted the cost of DESCRIBING them.
- **Profiled rather than guessed, and the dominant cost is pre-existing.** `index_identity` is ~6.7 s of the total: `COUNT(DISTINCT source_path)` 4,014 ms, `DISTINCT provider, model, dimension FROM embeddings` 2,956 ms (a `SCAN` plus temp B-tree over 739 MB to return one row), `chunk_id_set_sha` 418 ms — which matches its own docstring's "~0.5 s", so the claim that existed was accurate about the thing it described and the two dominant queries had simply never been characterised. `capture` has called this since 2026-09-17, so every snapshot has always paid it and `check.sh` gate 6 pays it every run. **Not fixed here:** the obvious remedy is an index on `embeddings`, and §11 forbids any index-format change in Z0. The embeddings query cannot become `LIMIT 1` either — the DISTINCT *is* the correctness check that catches a second embedding space.
- **Two design figures corrected downward and two upward; the flattering ones are not more true.** The wiki clone measures **43–72 ms** (median 47.3, n=5) against the design's `0.55–0.59 s` — one recursive `clonefile(2)` syscall rather than per-file `cp -c`, which forks 4,439 times. The graph clone is **0.33 ms**. Against that, **"zero disk" is retired**: a directory clone allocates ~4 MB of metadata for 4,439 inodes. The wording that survives measurement is *no additional file-data blocks were observed on APFS copy-on-write capture; filesystem metadata allocation is non-zero*.
- **Ranking neutrality is proven, not asserted.** The eval is byte-identical before and after (`diff` empty): Top-1 72%, Top-3 76%, MRR 0.74, 12 failures. What makes that worth anything is the pinned input: both runs stamp `chunk-set 0e71ee596e72`, recorded in Task 0 before a line changed. An identical score on a different corpus would prove nothing. The comparison was deliberately run **before** the design edit, because `docs/` is indexed — and the gate's own rebuild afterwards moved the corpus 185,173 -> 185,277 chunks, which would have made a later comparison uninterpretable.
- **Every test was falsified, and one falsification failed and had to be redone.** The immutability matrix, the read lock and the backend-equivalence contract all passed on first run, which proves nothing. Forcing a hardlink mechanism reddens the matrix; removing the read lock lets a concurrent writer commit where it otherwise gets `database is locked`; a divergent copy backend trips three equivalence tests. The first equivalence falsification was **inconclusive** — the injected fault raised `TypeError` rather than diverging, so it demonstrated only that the test fails when the harness explodes. Redone with a real content divergence.
- **The matrix inverts part of §4.5's reasoning.** Under a deliberately wrong mechanism only three of seven patterns fail: modify-in-place, truncate, write-through. Rename, delete, atomic-replace and tree-delete pass under BOTH mechanisms, because they change the directory entry and leave the inode alone. §4.5 argues those are the patterns a reviewer cannot predict — true, and they discriminate nothing. Recorded in the test file so nobody trims the three that matter.
- **Review findings adopted before execution, and one correction returned.** Six runtime defects that `test_plan_integrity.py` is structurally incapable of seeing: a multi-line signature that escaped a fixture rename, a manifest field that had moved, a check-then-clone race on the graph, `except OSError` treating ENOSPC as grounds to copy, `copytree(symlinks=True)` admitting a mutable out-of-snapshot path, and components captured under the final directory name so a SIGKILL left a valid-looking half-snapshot. The plan's "7 tasks" error was in my message, not the document. A compile pass over every Python block and an undeclared-import sweep were added to the pre-execution screen; they caught two missing imports.
- **A TDD violation I made and undid.** `clone_tree` and `assert_regular_tree` were written into Task 1's module before any test existed for them. Both were deleted, Task 2's tests written, all nine watched failing with `AttributeError`, then re-implemented. Keeping working code and calling it test-first is the exact rationalisation the law exists to stop.
- **Verification:** `check.sh` **all 24 gates, EXIT=0**, pytest inside it **1137 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed** in 257.87 s against a **1078-passed** floor recorded in Task 0, red-team **27/27**, `public_repo_guard` passed, `git diff --check` clean. `ls wiki/*.md` captured independently before and after the gate run: **byte-identical**. Live ledger CLEAN.
- **Merged and pushed at Raouf's explicit request.** `main` fast-forwarded to `02648c7` and pushed **`4d1c21f..02648c7`**, 12 commits — the 8 from `z0-step3-immutable-bytes` plus the two plan commits and the two that had been held local (`4541d1b`, `4b90e02`). Those two were listed before the push rather than swept along, because they leave the machine permanently. The merge was a fast-forward, and the delta between the gated tree (`c70ec6c`) and the merged tip is **documentation only** — verified by `git diff --name-only`, not assumed — so the pushed code is the code that ran the gates. The full suite was re-run on the merged tip anyway: **1137 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed**. Branch deleted; `git branch -d` would have refused had anything been unmerged. `main` is in sync with `origin/main`.
- **Written back to Zurvan through the ledger:** two decisions (capture cost is dominated by describing bytes, not freezing them; "zero disk" is retired), one claim (the matrix's five patterns are not equally discriminating), one open question (what Z1 should do about the 6.7 s `index_identity`). `claim add` refused the first evidence quote because I had paraphrased my own test file — the never-fabricate-citations rule firing on its author. The verbatim text was fetched rather than the quote loosened. Ledger CLEAN **revision 16**, implicit debt NONE. The index is now STALE by 8 files, and every measurement in this entry predates them.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Z0 §9.2 steps 4–7 remain; step 4 is atomic graph publication and carries gate 14's enforcement and gate 15. (2) `index_identity` costs ~6.7 s per capture and is unfixable inside Z0's no-index-change rule — belongs to Z1. (3) `zurvan snapshot list`/`gc` still collide with the release-archive command. (4) EXDEV on a second filesystem remains NOT MEASURED.

### 2026-09-19b (Australia/Sydney) — attribution split into four named facts, and the MCP path was violating the mutation barrier in production
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/knowledge_revision.py` (schema migration, four attribution fields, `declare_store_project`, ambient capture), `scripts/memory.py` (`add_decision` declares the subject it stamps), `scripts/cli.py` (wrapper stops passing the global selection; one help string corrected), `scripts/mcp_tools.py` (`mcp_operation` boundary on the four write tools), `scripts/knowledge_reconcile.py` (orphan report), `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-zurvan-2-amendments.md` (§8, amendments 5–6), two new test files, and four existing tests redirected off the live store (`test_mcp_tools.py`, `test_mcp_resources.py`, `test_context_export.py`). Amends Z0 step 1; **does not close Z0**.
- **The defect filed in 19a is fixed, and not the way that entry proposed.** 19a called it a one-line fix. It was not: the field was overloaded, so no single value could be correct. `mutations.project` is replaced by four columns, each named for what its value can actually support — `store_project` (the subject stamped on the page; the publication control `public_repo_guard` reads), `execution_project` (the registered project containing the working directory), `selected_project` (the registry's `current`, **diagnostic only**, never consulted for ownership, orphan attribution or routing), and `caller_project` (**reserved and deliberately always NULL** until a caller supplies a trustworthy project identity of its own).
- **`get_current_project()` is not a caller identity, and the repository already knew.** It reads the registry's `current`: one file shared by every concurrent session on the machine, so two sessions in two repos read the same value. `memory.resolve_project`'s docstring records the 2026-09-15 incident where exactly that mis-stamped four decisions written from inside the Zurvan repo. Naming it `caller_project` would have given a stale global a session-shaped name; it is recorded as `selected_project`, which is what it is.
- **The page and the ledger now take the subject from the same call.** `memory.add_decision` resolves it once, stamps the page with it and declares it to the active operation, so the disagreement 19a observed — page `zurvan`, ledger `otherproj` — is no longer representable. A second, *different* subject inside one operation raises: one mutation, one subject. Unreachable today (both `add_decision` call sites write one decision per operation), and pinned by a test so it stays that way.
- **The more urgent defect was found by reading the live ledger, not by reviewing code.** At revision 8 production held **four implicit mutations for two decisions**, alternating `scripts.memory:add_decision` and `scripts.memory:append_to_log`. `scripts/mcp_tools.py` called `memory` directly with no operation, so the page and the `wiki/log.md` append each activated their own mutation and **a revision was minted between them** — naming a state in which a decision page exists that the log does not mention. That is the half-operation the entire mutation barrier exists to forbid, running in production since the ledger went live. An MCP tool is now a top-level operation boundary, parallel to a CLI invocation; the decorator sits **inside** `enforce_read_only`, so a refused write never opens one.
- **The boundary is at the tool, not in `memory.add_decision`.** Making a library function its own operation would give every caller an independent boundary and move the split one level down. A memory function called with no boundary above it still becomes an implicit mutation, recorded as such for the migration inventory.
- **Migration of the live ledger invents nothing.** The recorded values ARE selections, so they move to `selected_project` verbatim; `store_project`, `execution_project` and `caller_project` stay NULL on historical rows, because nothing in the old row records those facts and a backfill would be a guess wearing the authoritative field's name. The migration re-reads `PRAGMA table_info` **inside** its transaction — `ensure_schema` runs on every mutation insert, so two processes can both see the old shape and the loser of the `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` race would otherwise fail the write that triggered it.
- **One deliberate departure from fail-closed, stated because it is a departure.** `execution_project` and `selected_project` are captured best-effort and land NULL on failure. They are diagnostics; a coherence ledger that refused a write because the project registry was unreadable would trade a real guarantee for a label. `store_project` is not best-effort — it comes from the same resolver the page uses.
- **A defect found and NOT fixed here, deliberately.** `zurvan decision add --project X` never reaches `add_decision(project=X)`: `cli.py:42` scans `sys.argv` for `--project` anywhere and pops it as the global workspace switch before argparse runs, then re-execs the CLI in that project's directory. Measured — the probe printed `Running against project: otherproj` and then `can't open file '/tmp/projtest/OTHERPROJ/scripts/cli.py'`. The subcommand's documented subject flag is shadowed. The help string now says so; fixing it changes CLI argument semantics and belongs in its own change. The cross-project divergence test was moved to the MCP surface, where it is reachable.
- **Stated cost.** Ambient attribution is **0.324 ms** per operation over a 5-project registry (measured, 200 iterations), paid once per boundary including read-only commands. Left eager rather than lazy so the capture happens at the boundary rather than at whatever the working directory has become by the first write.
- **A private project name leaked into tracked files, and the red-team gate caught it — including one occurrence already committed.** Writing this change up put the real client tag from `.private-projects` into `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` and the amendments spec, and `boundary_redteam.py`'s "no private project name in any tracked file" check failed (26/27). It is redacted to `otherproj` everywhere, in code comments, docstrings, tests and prose. **The 2026-09-19a entry carried the same leak and has been edited in place** — that entry was never pushed (`main` was `[ahead 1]`), so nothing left this machine, and a privacy redaction is the one kind of edit to a committed record this project makes: the wording is otherwise untouched and the name is simply gone. Recorded here so the edit is auditable rather than silent. The defect is also a reminder that 19a claimed verification from `public_repo_guard` and `git diff --check` alone — this gate lives in `check.sh`, which that entry did not run.
- **The gate went red on something none of this touched, and the root cause was four PRODUCTION-STORE ISOLATION VIOLATIONS — not test-cleanup bugs.** `check.sh` failed at the session guard: `wiki pages: 120 -> 119`, a page DELETED from the live store, with no write attribution. It did not reproduce on a clean re-run (1073 passed, live `wiki/*.md` byte-identical before and after), which was the clue rather than the answer. `tests/test_mcp_tools.py`, `tests/test_mcp_resources.py` and two tests in `tests/test_context_export.py` each wrote a **fixed-name** page into the LIVE `wiki/` and `os.remove`d it. The count comes back even when a run completes, so a count-based guard never sees it — but a run killed between the write and the remove leaves the page behind, and the NEXT run then deletes a page it never created. Two of my own killed runs earlier in this session supplied exactly that stray. `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::test_tool_zurvan_remember` had already been fixed for this reason and carries the note "cleanup is not isolation"; these four were missed. All four now redirect `PROJECT_ROOT` to `tmp_path`, and a sweep confirms no test names a live relative path any more. The guard was not touched — it was right, and it caught a real production-touching defect that had been latent since before this change.
  **Stated this way deliberately:** these were not tests that forgot to tidy up. They were tests operating on the production knowledge store, whose cleanup happened to MASK the violation on any uninterrupted run. Anyone later tempted to "simplify" them back to a live path with an `os.remove` at the end would be re-arming exactly this trap, and the mask is the dangerous part: a run that completes looks innocent.
- **Verification, run and read rather than predicted.** `check.sh` **all 24 gates green, EXIT=0** ("All Zurvan checks passed successfully"), pytest inside it **1073 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed** in 238.53 s against a 1046-passed baseline, red-team **27/27**, `public_repo_guard` passed (re-run fresh afterwards too), `git diff --check` clean. An INDEPENDENT check of the guard's own subject: `ls wiki/*.md` captured before and after the gate run, **byte-identical** — the live store was not touched by the suite. Live ledger **CLEAN revision 12 / MANAGED / baseline recorded**.
- **Migration debt closed without destroying the evidence.** The four historical implicit rows are **preserved** (`deleted: 0`) and marked RESOLVED with `actor=Raouf` and a reason naming what became true: all known compound entry points now establish an operation boundary — CLI already wrapped, MCP wrapped here — and a call-site audit first confirmed `append_to_log` has no independent unwrapped caller (4 callers, all inside `memory.py`'s `add_*`; those have 8 call sites, 4 in `cli.py` under the wrapper and 4 in `mcp_tools.py` under `@mcp_operation`; `scripts/zurvan` execs `cli.py` and line 1146 is the only call to `_main`). `zurvan knowledge callers` now reports **NONE**. The claim is self-falsifying: a later implicit write from the same caller reappears in the inventory by itself. Those rows stay because they are evidence the invariant was once violated in production and then repaired.
- **The fix proven on real production writes, not fixtures.** The four write-backs recorded `store_project=zurvan`, `execution_project=zurvan`, `selected_project=<the stale global selection>`, `caller_project=NULL`, `writer=cli`, `implicit=False`, two completed paths each, one revision each (8 -> 12). The decision page the first of them wrote stamps `project: "zurvan"` — **ledger and page now agree**, where on 2026-09-19 they did not. The claim and question rows correctly carry `store_project=NULL`: those page types stamp no `project:` field, so the subject is genuinely unresolvable and is recorded as absent rather than guessed.
- **The write-backs made the search index stale, as they always do.** `6 file(s) newer than the index`; they were run AFTER every measurement in this entry, so no number above depends on the corpus that moved. Rebuild with `zurvan index search` — deliberately not run here, because a rebuild during a session whose gates have already been recorded would invalidate nothing but would leave the next reader unable to tell.
- **Contracts:** 16 attribution (schema, migration, field semantics, two end-to-end regressions), 11 MCP boundary. Two existing `Operation(project=...)` constructions updated to `store_project=`.

### 2026-09-18j (Australia/Sydney) — Z0 step 1 shipped: a revision is a logical operation, and execution found five defects three gauntlets missed
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/knowledge_scope.py`, `knowledge_manifest.py`, `knowledge_revision.py`, `knowledge_reconcile.py` (all new), `scripts/safe_write.py` (two-phase participation), `scripts/cli.py` (`_main` + wrapper, `zurvan knowledge` group), `scripts/measurement_snapshot.py` (delegates its manifest), `tests/conftest.py` (the guard watches the ledger), four new test files plus `tests/test_conftest_guard_behaviour.py`. 15 commits on `z0-step1-knowledge-revisions`, 2,596 insertions. Implements §9.2 steps 1–2 of the Z0 design.
- **This does NOT close Z0.** The gate (§10) remains unmet: no immutable-bytes abstraction, no wiki or graph capture, no atomic graph rebuild, no `built_from_revision`, no two-purpose capture, no leases. `measurement_snapshot.py` still pins only `data/search.sqlite`. Steps 3–4 and 5–7 have not been planned yet.
- **A revision is advanced by a logical operation, never by a file write.** `decision add` writes a page and appends `wiki/log.md`; a revision minted between them is a half-operation wearing a valid number. Writes are two-phase — `plan_write` before the bytes, `complete_write` after — and the mutation row activates lazily on the first PLANNED write, so `zurvan search` leaves the ledger untouched and cannot make a capture report BUSY. `_stamp`/`writelog` stays best-effort telemetry; the coherence ledger fails closed.
- **The verification, run and read rather than predicted:** `check.sh` **all 24 gates**, pytest inside it **1046 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed** in 120.69 s against a **951-passed baseline**; gate 6 reports `185087 chunks / 4479 sources chunk-set 2b12a589a8a8` in `sentence_transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2/384`, so the exact-provider invariant is intact and retrieval did not move; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. The live-store guard stayed silent, and **production `data/registry.sqlite` still holds exactly `['sources', 'sqlite_sequence']`** after the full run — the contamination regression verified against the real store, not a fixture. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Contracts:** 6 scope, 9 manifest, 63 revision, 11 reconcile, 11 guard-behaviour (4 pre-existing, unchanged). The 14 existing `measurement_snapshot` contracts were not rewritten.
- **Five defects that three rounds of gauntleting did not catch, because reading is not running.**
- **(1) The repository already had a gate for what I was hand-checking.** The baseline suite failed on my own plan: `test_no_deferred_markers` found `TBD` inside the self-review sentence denying it. Worse, that test splits on `## Task N` and my headings were `### Task N:`, so `test_every_task_shows_code` saw **zero tasks and passed without examining anything**. Fixed the document, never the checks — and three gauntlets had grepped for those markers by hand while a real gate sat unrun.
- **(2) A new SQLite module broke an invariant this file already records.** `_connect` created the database on a READ. `AGENTS.md` states it for the search index — readers open `mode=ro` because `sqlite3.connect` creates what it cannot find — and I wrote a new store that ignored it. Caught by smoking `zurvan knowledge status` against a virgin store, not by 57 green tests.
- **(3) "No ledger" has three shapes and fixtures reach only two.** No registry file; a registry with the full schema; and — production's actual state — a registry holding `sources` from ingestion that has never taken a ledger write. In the third the connection opens fine and the QUERY fails. `doctor`, `callers`, `mutations_in` and `implicit_mutations` all tracebacked against the real store while every test passed.
- **(4) A test was asserting against a stranger's file.** `import tests.conftest` resolves to `/opt/anaconda3/.../site-packages/tests/conftest.py`, an unrelated package that owns the name. Had it defined `_snapshot`, the test would have passed while checking nothing about this repository. It now loads by explicit path and asserts the path.
- **(5) The pleasant-sounding answer was the misleading one.** `zurvan knowledge callers` printed "No unresolved implicit callers" on a store with no ledger — which reads as *migration complete* when the truth is *nothing has been recorded*. It now says which. This one mattered because the plan's own closure step asks me to confirm that inventory before claiming migration.
- **Two smaller ones, recorded because they were mine.** Task 7's tests called `implicit_mutations()`, which the plan defined in Task 8, so its advertised count was unreachable as ordered — the helper was pulled forward. And a `git commit -m` containing backticks ran command substitution and embedded ImageMagick's usage text into a commit message, amended via heredoc.
- **One test assertion corrected, and the distinction matters.** `_describe_caller` correctly recorded `test_knowledge_revision:test_...`; the assertion `"knowledge_revision" not in caller` failed because the **test module itself** is named that. The mechanism was right and the assertion was naive, so the assertion changed — the checker never gets loosened to make evidence pass.
- **The ledger is NOT yet active in production.** Every write during this work went to a throwaway root, so the real store still has no `knowledge_state`. `zurvan knowledge status` says so. It will be created by the first real write through `safe_write`, or by `zurvan knowledge bootstrap`, and until then drift detection has no baseline to contradict — which is exactly why bootstrap is a deliberate command rather than an implicit side effect.
- **Stated cost.** The authoritative manifest is **31 ms** over 4,405 files (`os.walk` with a structural prune; the `Path.rglob` + per-file `resolve()` shape the plan originally specified measured 464 ms), plus a cached 3.7 ms `ps` per process. Read-only commands pay neither. The suite is **~90 s slower** because four CLI contracts run in a subprocess — `memory.py` binds `PROJECT_ROOT` at import, so an in-process CLI test cannot be redirected and would silently exercise nothing.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Plans for §9.2 steps 3–4 and 5–7. (2) `zurvan snapshot list`/`gc` collide with the release-archive command and belong with Plan 2's code. (3) Bootstrap the production ledger deliberately when Raouf chooses. (4) The concurrent-mutation manifest bound (§5.2) stands unmeasured.

### 2026-09-18i (Australia/Sydney) — third gauntlet: the plan's own cost claim was 15× wrong and a test could not detect what it tested
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** documentation only. `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-18-z0-step1-knowledge-revisions.md` (rev 3, complete rewrite — 2,319 → 3,354 lines, 11 → 14 tasks, 63 → 79 steps), `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-z0-coherent-snapshots-design.md` and `...-amendments.md` (cost claims corrected). No code, no tests, no gates touched.
- **Rev 2 was reviewed line by line and needed re-architecting, not patching.** Thirteen findings, several structural. The write lifecycle was the root of three: rev 2 hooked only `_stamp`, which runs **after** publication, so a crash between the bytes landing and the bookkeeping left `planned_paths` and `completed_paths` both empty — and orphan detection then concluded `ABORTED` and left the store `CLEAN` while the file had already changed. That is precisely the crash `planned_paths` was invented for. Writes are now two-phase: `plan_write` before, `complete_write` after, and the ledger **fails closed** where `writelog` may stay best-effort telemetry.
- **Reconciliation was laundering, by the approved design's own definition.** Rev 2 set `state = CLEAN` in place, leaving `revision 14 / pre-orphan manifest / CLEAN`. The design (§3.5) requires a NEW CLEAN revision. It now mints one with a fresh manifest, transitions orphans to a terminal `RESOLVED`, and writes a durable `reconciliations` row recording who accepted what, when, why, and which manifests they inspected — because "someone passed `--accept`" is not provenance.
- **Making the mutation activate lazily fixed two red findings with one change.** The row is created on the first PLANNED write, so `zurvan search` writes nothing to the ledger, takes no write lock, and cannot make a capture report BUSY — while `planned_paths` still lands before any bytes do.
- **Then I gauntleted rev 3 and found eight more, three red, all by executing its assumptions.**
- **The plan's own headline cost number was wrong by 15×.** The design's "19 ms" manifest was measured with `os.walk` over a pre-built list; the plan specified `Path.rglob` plus a `resolve()` per file, which measures **238 ms to list and 226 ms to hash — 464 ms** against **31/31/30 ms** for a walk with a structural prune. `commit_mutation` runs this on every writing command. The mechanism is only ever as cheap as the walk that feeds it, so the implementation is now specified rather than left to the implementer. Honest cost of the feature: **~35 ms per writing command**, not the ~23 ms rev 2 claimed.
- **A test could not detect the thing it tested.** Reconciliation was told to reuse `graph_build.parse_frontmatter` rather than add a third definition of "valid frontmatter" — correct, and it **never raises**: torn frontmatter returns `({}, '---\ntitle: [unclosed\n')`, identical in shape to a file with no frontmatter at all. Detection now uses the structural signal — the file opens a block the parser did not consume — which still treats the shared parser as the definition of what Zurvan can read.
- **Four CLI tests could never have run.** `scripts/memory.py:7` binds `PROJECT_ROOT` at import, so an in-process `decision add` targets the real wiki; `safe_write.is_safe_path` then refuses the write (failing safe, at least) and the revision never advances. They now run in a subprocess, which reads the environment at import — the same reason `tests/test_cli.py:25` already shells out.
- **Two more tests passed for the wrong reason.** On a fresh store `observe_external_drift` raises the bootstrap error before reaching its in-flight and SUSPECT checks, so neither branch was exercised; both tests now bootstrap first and assert on the message. A third called `memory.add_note` in-process and wrapped its assertions in `if rows:`, vacuous in both directions — deleted, since the following test proves the property properly.
- **Corrections to already-published figures.** The live design and amendments now carry 31 ms; the earlier `AGENTS.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` entries are **left exactly as written**. They record what was believed when they were written, and this project keeps receipts rather than rewriting them.
- **Verification:** 14 tasks, 79 steps; every `kr.*`, `ks.*`, `km.*` and `krec.*` symbol used by a test is defined by an earlier task (checked mechanically, zero undefined); every advertised `Expected: N passed` matches the cumulative test count in that file exactly — `[6, 11, 13, 26, 31, 35, 44, 53, 57]` for the revision suite, 9 / 11 / 6 / 7 for the others; no placeholder patterns; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. No pytest claim — nothing executable changed. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **The scorecard is recorded, not replaced.** Rev 1 scored 2/10 and 3/10 on the two axes that mattered; rev 2 raised both to 8 and was then found unsafe against crashes, non-conforming on reconciliation, and carrying two unreachable test counts. **No line of this plan has executed.** Every score remains a claim about a document.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Execute the plan. (2) Plans for §9.2 steps 3–4 and 5–7. (3) `zurvan snapshot list`/`gc` naming resolution belongs with Plan 2's code.

### 2026-09-18h (Australia/Sydney) — I gauntleted my own plan and it failed: four red findings, three of which would have damaged production
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** documentation only. `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-18-z0-step1-knowledge-revisions.md` (rev 2, 1,950 → 2,317 lines, 10 → 11 tasks, 55 → 63 steps, gauntlet appendix added), `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-z0-coherent-snapshots-design.md` (§4.1, §3.2, §5.1, §5.2 amended). No code, no tests, no gates touched.
- **Ten findings, every one produced by running something rather than re-reading.** Four red, three amber, three yellow. The plan was **not executable as written** and is recorded that way rather than quietly repaired.
- **G1 — the plan's ledger would have written into the production store during tests, invisibly.** `scripts.config.PROJECT_ROOT` is bound at import; `safe_write.get_project_root()` reads the environment at call time. Measured with the module imported first, exactly as pytest does it: `config.PROJECT_ROOT` → the real repository while `safe_write` correctly followed `/tmp/fake-store`. So the page under test lands in the redirect and the ledger lands in **production `data/registry.sqlite`** — and `tests/conftest.py` watches `wiki/`, not `data/`, so nothing fires. This is the class AGENTS.md attributes to three separate 2026-09-17 failures, and I reintroduced it.
- **G2 — routine CLI failures would have marked the shared store SUSPECT.** `sys.exit` raises `SystemExit`, a `BaseException`, and `mutation()` caught `BaseException` to abandon. `scripts/cli.py` calls `sys.exit(1)` on roughly twenty dispatch branches. The sharpest case: `zurvan knowledge reconcile` exits 1 on `NEEDS_REVIEW`, so it would have SUSPECTed the store it had just cleared. `SystemExit` is now handled separately — commit on code 0/None, abandon otherwise, with the ABORTED/ORPHANED split still decided by whether anything was written.
- **G3/G4 — the CLI task could not have run at all.** Extracting `_dispatch(args, parser)` raises `NameError`: the dispatch body closes over `trace_parser` (835), `boundary_parser` (894), `graph_parser` (1010) and `snapshot_parser` (1043), all locals created before `parse_args`. And `def main():` takes **no arguments** — the plan called `main([...])`. The fix is a one-line rename to `_main` plus a six-line wrapper, with tests patching `sys.argv` as the existing suite already does.
- **G5 — Zurvan's own output would have registered as external tampering.** `scripts/trace_writer.py:20` writes `wiki/traces/` with `write_text`, bypassing `safe_write` and the barrier, so every traced command would mint an `EXTERNAL_OBSERVED` revision — the drift mechanism firing on the thing it exists to detect. The clone stays a superset for forensics; the authoritative manifest now excludes derived subtrees. Measured: 25 of 4,430 files, 0.6%; manifest 19/19/18 ms excluding traces against 26/20/19 including.
- **G6 — cross-project writes would have gone untracked, failing open.** `zurvan --project X` re-invokes the CLI against a different store with `ZURVAN_MUTATION_ID` still inherited, so `active_id()` reads truthy, `record_write` finds no matching row and drops the write, and the implicit fallback never fires either. An inherited id is now honoured only when it names an ACTIVE row in the store being written.
- **G7, G8, G9, G10 — three fixes and one bound.** Writes outside `wiki/` no longer advance a revision (`is_safe_path` permits everything under the root except `raw/`). `isolation_level=None` removes a latent transaction class: DML-then-`BEGIN IMMEDIATE` raises `cannot start a transaction within a transaction`, verified, and every current path happens to begin first — which is how that kind ships. `process_identity` forked `ps` at **3.7 ms** per mutation and is now cached per process. And the concurrency case is **stated as a bound rather than engineered away**: when two mutations are in flight, the first to commit records a manifest already containing the other's half-written files; serialising every operation across a shared cross-project store would reintroduce the global lock this design exists to avoid.
- **The 10/10 delta was built, not just named.** New Task 10 extends `tests/conftest.py`'s existing guard to watch the revision ledger, so any future module that resolves a root wrongly trips the same wire that already catches a stray page. Its own regression runs through the nested-pytest harness that `test_conftest_guard_behaviour.py` already uses, and its step 5 says plainly: if the notice appears, investigate — **do not silence it**.
- **Measured cost of the feature, stated:** ~23 ms per writing CLI command (19 ms manifest + 3.7 ms `ps`, now once per process). Read-only commands pay neither, because a mutation that wrote nothing computes no manifest and commits no revision.
- **The scorecard went down and stays down.** Rev 1 scored 2/10 on correctness-under-test-isolation and 3/10 on survives-the-real-CLI. Both are fixed, but the appendix records the original scores rather than replacing them — a scorecard that only goes up has stopped measuring.
- **Verification:** 11 tasks, 63 checkbox steps, no placeholder patterns, no stale `_dispatch` instructions (the two remaining mentions document why not to do it); `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. No pytest claim — nothing executable changed. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Execute the plan. (2) Plans for §9.2 steps 3–4 and 5–7. (3) `zurvan snapshot list`/`gc` naming resolution belongs with Plan 2's code.

### 2026-09-18g (Australia/Sydney) — Z0 step 1 planned: 10 tasks, 55 TDD steps, and a naming collision caught before it was made twice
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** documentation only. `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-18-z0-step1-knowledge-revisions.md` (new, 1,950 lines), `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-z0-coherent-snapshots-design.md` (publication invariant tightened to Raouf's wording). No code, no tests, no gates touched.
- **Scoped to §9.2 steps 1–2 rather than all seven.** The plan produces working revision tracking and orphan recovery while `measurement_snapshot.py` behaves exactly as it does today — no snapshot, retrieval or index code is touched. Steps 3–4 and 5–7 get their own plans in that order. **Z0 step 1 does not close Z0**, and the plan's last task requires the change entry to say so and enumerate what is still missing.
- **The write surface turned out to be three modules, not a codebase-wide sweep.** `grep` for `write_file_safely|append_file_safely` finds `memory.py` (6 sites), `wiki_merge.py` (3) and the definitions themselves. Every CLI path reaches them through `main()`, so wrapping the dispatcher covers all of them and any added later — which is why the plan wraps there rather than rewriting nine call sites.
- **`zurvan snapshot doctor` would have collided, and this repository has made that exact mistake before.** `zurvan snapshot` already builds release archives and `scripts/snapshot.py` is its module; the previous session had to rename a new module to `measurement_snapshot.py` for the same reason, and recorded it precisely so a future reader would not repeat it. The plan uses `zurvan knowledge status|doctor|reconcile` and Task 9 corrects the design doc. `zurvan snapshot list`/`gc` collide too and are left for the Plan 2 author, because that decision belongs with the code that implements them.
- **Two behaviours the plan settles that the design left to implementation.** (1) An exception inside a mutation abandons rather than commits, and the choice between `ABORTED` and `ORPHANED` is made by whether anything was actually written — a command that failed on its arguments tore nothing, and marking the whole store SUSPECT for it would make the signal worthless within a day. (2) A mutation that wrote nothing commits no revision, because the dispatcher wraps read-only commands too and a revision per `zurvan search` would make the counter measure traffic rather than knowledge.
- **Verified before planning, not assumed:** no test asserts a literal manifest digest (`tests/test_measurement_snapshot.py:111,122` check presence and cross-snapshot equality only), so extending `_corpus_manifest_sha` with `st_ino` changes its value once without breaking a contract. `psutil` is not installed, so process identity uses `ps -p PID -o lstart=`, confirmed working. `registry.sqlite` holds only `sources`, so both new tables are additive.
- **One rough edge flagged in the plan rather than hidden.** Task 8's dispatcher wrap passes a project expression that always evaluates to `None`; it is a placeholder for `project_registry.get_current_project()`, and the self-review says so and tells the implementer to replace it and test it. On a shared cross-project store, `(unknown)` in an orphan report is a worse diagnostic than it looks.
- **Verification:** 10 tasks, 55 checkbox steps, task headers unique, no placeholder patterns; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. No pytest claim — nothing executable changed. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Execute this plan. (2) Plans for §9.2 steps 3–4 and 5–7. (3) `zurvan snapshot list`/`gc` naming resolution in Plan 2.

### 2026-09-18f (Australia/Sydney) — design review found two deadlocks, an unsafe compatibility path and a blind spot; rev 2 of the Z0 design
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** documentation only. `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-z0-coherent-snapshots-design.md` (rev 2, 544 → 794 lines), `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-zurvan-2-amendments.md` (amendment 2 extended, manifest wording corrected). No code, no tests, no gates touched.
- **Four findings, all verified against the text as written rather than accepted on description.** Rev 1 was wrong, not unclear, in each case: `design:107-110` claimed implicit mutations preserved atomicity; `design:373-374` had capture verify derived stamps while `design:313` had rebuild begin by capturing; `knowledge_state` bound no manifest to a revision; reconcile steps 6–7 required a rebuild that `design:326` makes refuse SUSPECT input.
- **Two of the four were the same bug wearing two hats.** One capture contract served two jobs. Rebuild needs *inputs*; measurement needs *inputs plus proven derived stores*. Conflated, a stale search index could never be rebuilt — capture refused because search was stale, rebuild needed capture — and reconciliation could never reach CLEAN, because CLEAN required a rebuild that refuses SUSPECT. Split into an **authoritative** capture (`required={"wiki"}`, inspects no derived store) and a **measurement** capture (`required` declared at the call site), both deadlocks dissolve. `CLEAN` is now defined as a property of authoritative state, never of derived currency, which is what `CURRENT`/`STALE`/`INVALID` already model.
- **The compatibility path recreated the exact defect it sat next to.** An unwrapped caller doing two writes commits an implicit mutation after each, so a capture between them sees no ACTIVE mutation and succeeds on a half-operation. Implicit mutation is now a **migration mechanism only**: warn, record the caller, wrap at the top-level CLI dispatcher so existing commands inherit a context without rewriting call sites, and close Z0 only when a query over `mutations.implicit` shows no production multi-write operation relies on it. Z0 may not claim atomicity for an unknown caller.
- **The double manifest caught races and was blind to finished edits.** An Obsidian edit that *completes* before capture leaves `M_before == M_after` perfectly stable while the derived stores were built from the old bytes — coherence declared falsely, and nothing in rev 1 could see it. Stability and awareness are different questions. Every revision now records the authoritative manifest digest it represents, and capture compares live against it. Drift does **not** mark SUSPECT — ordinary manual editing is a supported workflow — it mints a new CLEAN revision with `atomicity = EXTERNAL_OBSERVED`, which claims one stable observed filesystem state and explicitly **not** one logical user transaction. Derived stores then read correctly as STALE.
- **`planned_paths` before the writes, `completed_paths` after each publication.** A single list stamped after the fact loses exactly the path reconciliation needs when a process dies between writing a file and stamping it. The difference between the two lists *is* the investigation. Reconcile also now **stops for human review when semantic completeness cannot be established** — a decision page can parse perfectly while `log.md` never received its line, and Markdown syntax is not evidence that a two-file operation finished.
- **I pushed back on the WAL finding and measured instead of arguing.** The premise — that `search.sqlite` may not be the complete database — is **false here**: all three stores report `journal_mode=delete` and no `-wal`, `-shm` or `-journal` sidecar exists. So it enters as an **assertion locking in a property that currently holds**, not as a fix for a live defect, because calling it a fix would misreport the state. It still earns its place: nothing asserts `journal_mode`, so a future `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` would silently invalidate every snapshot ever taken, and the graph is written in place today and can carry a journal sidecar mid-write.
- **A wording defect worth naming.** Rev 1 said `_corpus_manifest_sha` already computes `(relpath, size, mtime_ns, inode)`. Ground truth at `measurement_snapshot.py:116` is `f"{rel}\x00{st.st_size}\x00{st.st_mtime_ns}"` — **no inode**. My own ad-hoc measurement script included it and I conflated the two, describing new behaviour as existing. Both documents now say the extension is new; cost is unchanged because the same `stat` call already returns `st_ino`.
- **Gate grew from 12 to 20 named tests**, including no-deadlock (stale search → authoritative capture → rebuild → measurement capture all succeed), completed-unmanaged-edit detection, atomicity not laundered through a snapshot, reconcile reaching CLEAN without a rebuild, reconcile stopping for review, planned-vs-completed recovery, and the `journal_mode` assertion.
- **Verification:** all rev-1 contradictions confirmed absent from the text; both documents agree on the manifest tuple; no placeholders; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. No pytest claim — nothing executable changed. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) `writing-plans` once rev 2 is approved. (2) Open question added: if `EXTERNAL_OBSERVED` fires constantly in daily Obsidian use, the manifest check is measuring normal life rather than drift and the workflow needs a cheaper way to tell Zurvan an edit happened.

### 2026-09-18e (Australia/Sydney) — Z0 designed as the derived-store lifecycle; a snapshot that cannot prove coherence refuses to exist
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** documentation only. `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-z0-coherent-snapshots-design.md` (new, 544 lines). No code, no tests, no gates touched.
- **The defect Z0 exists to remove is invisible at the call site.** A snapshot with a frozen index and a moving wiki answers questions exactly like a coherent one — the same shape as the 8,000-of-185,303-chunk index: structurally valid, silently wrong. So the gate is not "capture more files", it is **a snapshot refuses to exist when it cannot prove one coherent knowledge state**.
- **A revision is advanced by a logical mutation, never by a file write.** The obvious design increments inside `safe_write` and is wrong for an ordinary reason: `decision add` writes the page and appends `log.md`, so a snapshot between them is byte-perfect and logically half-finished. `begin_mutation`/`end_mutation` wrap at CLI command level, cross-process because the store is shared; `safe_write` becomes a participant through the `_stamp` → `writelog.record` path that already carries session token, pid and ppid. A write with no active mutation is an implicit single-write mutation, not an error — anything stricter breaks every existing caller for a property that is preserved either way.
- **Liveness and trustworthiness are separate facts.** A dead owner proves the mutation will not finish and proves nothing about whether its bytes are complete, so rollback would be fiction and timeout-commit a lie of the same size. Orphaned mutation → revision `SUSPECT` → snapshots of it refused while live writes continue. **SUSPECT propagates**: if R held a half-written page and the next mutation only appends a log, R+1 still holds it. Cleared only by `zurvan knowledge reconcile`, which may refuse and report but may never resolve by deleting.
- **`built_from_revision` is unimplementable honestly unless rebuilds read snapshots.** A rebuild starting at R while the corpus reaches R+3 indexed no single revision. Both paths become `rebuild_search_index(snapshot=S)` / `graph_build(snapshot=S)` with no implicit live reads once the build starts, and a rebuild **refuses a SUSPECT snapshot** — otherwise reconciliation is bypassable by laundering torn state through a derived store until it looks internally consistent.
- **Three health states, not two:** `COHERENT+CURRENT`, `COHERENT+STALE`, `INVALID`. Promotion deliberately does **not** require CURRENT, because a busy store advances while the rebuild runs and that requirement means a sufficiently active Zurvan never finishes a rebuild.
- **The double manifest is a correctness mechanism, not a diagnostic.** The barrier cannot cover Obsidian, an editor or `cp`, and such an edit changes neither the revision nor the mutation flag — so re-reading both detects nothing. `_corpus_manifest_sha` already computes the right shape at 15–17 ms warm and gets promoted. **Bound stated rather than engineered away:** size + `mtime_ns` + inode is not a content hash, so an in-place same-size write inside one nanosecond tick is undetectable; a 135 MB content hash per capture is the alternative and no observed failure justifies it.
- **Two invariants written down because breaking them would be silent.** (1) A hardlink snapshot is valid only for a store whose published inode is never modified in place — true of `search.sqlite` only because rebuild promotes via `os.replace()`; Z0 owes a regression that attempts an in-place production write and fails loudly. (2) The wiki immutability test must span modify, rename, delete, atomic-replace and truncate, because proving inode separation under one edit pattern proves too little.
- **Twelve-item gate, each with a named test**, including the canonical §8.7 A/B isolation experiment, a forced write *during* capture, a snapshot attempted between `decision add`'s two writes, and an orphan-kill that must leave writes available while refusing snapshots. Plus a measured capture cost against today's 485 ms — **the ~1.1 s figure in the design is an estimate, and an estimate is not a result.**
- **Verification:** all ten cited `file:line` references verified against the tree; the 14 existing snapshot contracts confirmed; `.building-<pid>` + `os.replace()` confirmed in `rebuild_search_index.py`; no placeholders; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. No pytest claim — nothing executable changed. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Implementation plan via writing-plans, pending Raouf's review of the design. (2) The 83% vector finding still needs a `zurvan claim add` write-back. (3) Z1's ≥350-query instrument remains unscoped.

### 2026-09-18d (Australia/Sydney) — the Zurvan 2.0 programme enters the repo, with an honest statement of what is not built
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** documentation only. `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-zurvan-2-canonical-upgrade-spec.md` (new, verbatim), `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-zurvan-2-amendments.md` (new). No code, no tests, no gates touched.
- **The canonical architecture spec existed only on the Desktop.** 2,777 lines, two adversarial gauntlet passes, 67 findings, phases Z0–Z12. Committed byte-identical (`diff -q` clean) so its lineage survives; every change since lives in the amendments file instead of being edited into a document labelled FINAL.
- **Z0 is recorded as PARTIAL, roughly one of seven components — not closed.** `measurement_snapshot.py` pins `data/search.sqlite` and nothing else. `grep -rn 'knowledge_revision\|built_from_revision\|generations/' scripts/ tests/` returns nothing: no revision, no capture barrier, no wiki byte replay, no graph capture, no claim-ledger hook, no lease/GC, no capture-race retry. The Z0 gate is §30.16, cross-store revision coherence; what exists proves per-file immutability for one file. Written down because the alternative is a future session reading "snapshots: done".
- **Four amendments, each forced by a measurement rather than a preference.** (1) **Z1V** inserted after Z1 for the measured 83% vector scan, gated by §9.5 unchanged — approximation does not win on latency alone. (2) **Z0 widened** to the derived-store lifecycle including snapshot-backed search and graph rebuilds, because `built_from_revision` is otherwise unimplementable honestly: a rebuild reading the live wiki while the corpus moves R → R+3 indexed no single revision and stamping either number is false. (3) **`data/generations/` replaced** by a portable immutable-snapshot abstraction — clonefile preferred, byte copy as the correctness fallback, no bespoke CAS. (4) **Claim-ledger component declared unsupported** with a fail-closed guard, because `claims.sqlite` arrives in Z2 and Z0 must not claim a capability it lacks.
- **The mechanism amendment is measured, and its bound is stated.** On this APFS volume: cloning all of `wiki/` (135 MB, 4,425 files) is **0.55–0.59 s** across three runs and consumes **zero blocks** — `du` reports 417 MB for three clones while `df` free space *rose* 5.5 MB. `graph.sqlite` clones in under 10 ms; the existing `os.link` on the 738 MB index is 0.26 ms; a stat-walk manifest over 4,425 files is 15–17 ms warm, 116 ms cold. Clones carry distinct inodes at `nlink` 1, so a live in-place edit provably cannot reach them. **Bound: one APFS volume, one machine.** `cp -c` fails across volumes and does not exist off APFS, which is why the design is written portable-first rather than as an APFS design.
- **A hardlink to `data/graph.sqlite` would have been a silent determinism failure.** `scripts/graph_build.py:186` is a bare `sqlite3.connect(db_path)` on the canonical path — no build-and-promote — so the graph inode is mutated in place and A35 forbids linking it. Found by reading the write path rather than by trusting the search index's precedent; the amendment makes the graph rebuild atomic instead of working around it.
- **Verification:** `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean; canonical copy byte-identical to source. No pytest claim is made — nothing executable changed. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Z0 completion design, next in this session. (2) The 83% vector finding still needs a `zurvan claim add` write-back so it is searchable, not only in docs. (3) Z1's ≥350-query instrument remains unscoped.

### 2026-09-18c (Australia/Sydney) — measurements read a frozen index; the shared brain stays writable
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/measurement_snapshot.py` (new), `scripts/writelog.py` (new), `scripts/safe_write.py` (stamping), `scripts/eval_search.py` (`--index` + reported identity), `scripts/check.sh` (gate 6 scores a snapshot; orphan sweep), `tests/conftest.py` (the guard now asks whose write it was), `tests/test_measurement_snapshot.py` + `tests/test_write_attribution.py` + `tests/test_conftest_guard_behaviour.py` (new, 31 contracts), Boundary-adjacent none. Implements the design committed at `62100e5`.
- **The invariant:** shared knowledge may remain live and writable; measurements must not depend on the shared store remaining stationary. Freezing Zurvan to measure Zurvan was the wrong lever.
- **The mechanism is cheap because of something already true.** A rebuild builds into a temp file and promotes with one `os.replace()`, so the canonical path gets a NEW inode and old bytes are never mutated in place. A hard link taken beforehand keeps pointing at exactly those bytes. **`os.link` 0.26 ms; `capture()` 485 ms end to end** (386 ms of it the chunk-id-set hash that gives the snapshot an identity); **no disk cost** — `nlink` 1→2, same inode; peak memory 1.20 MB. `deep=True` hashes all 738 MB of vectors for +743 ms and is opt-in for that reason.
- **No measurable retrieval overhead, and the same numbers.** Canonical vs snapshot: top-1 **0.72**, top-3 **0.76**, MRR **0.7423**, **12** failures, same failing queries, same `chunk_id_set_sha`. My first comparison suggested **+62% snapshot overhead** and was wrong — sequential ordering noise. Interleaved it is −2.5% with an 84%-of-median spread *within* each condition, so the honest statement is no detectable difference, which is what the same inode predicts.
- **The design's premise was right but incomplete, and the evidence said so.** §3 correctly noted that `eval_search --hybrid` reads only the index — so freezing one file freezes the measurement. But **both real aborts were in gate 2, pytest** (2026-09-17 14:57, 2026-09-18 15:21), not the retrieval evaluation. Pytest is not scoring retrieval; it asserts the suite did not contaminate the corpus. Snapshots alone would have prevented **neither**.
- **So §6 is answered rather than deferred: the guard stays and asks a better question.** Writes to `wiki/` are stamped at the `safe_write` choke point with pid, ppid and a session token inherited through the environment; the guard fails for writes from its **own process tree**, including subprocesses — the 48-stray-pages defect, unchanged — and records external writes without failing. It **fails closed**: `all_external([])` is False, so a new write path that forgets to stamp makes the guard stricter, never weaker. Attribution is by inheritance, never by message text: "does this look like a test note?" is wrong in both directions.
- **The guard's own behaviour is proven, not argued.** `tests/test_conftest_guard_behaviour.py` runs a nested pytest over a COPY of `conftest.py` in a temp directory, so `LIVE_ROOT` resolves there and production is never in scope: ours → fails, external → passes with a visible notice, unattributed → fails. It caught a real defect immediately — the external notice was invisible in two successive attempts because **pytest captures file descriptor 2**, so neither `print` nor `sys.__stderr__` survives a passing run. It now goes through the terminal reporter. A diagnostic nobody sees is not a diagnostic.
- **I overwrote an existing module and the suite caught it.** The design named `scripts/snapshot.py`; that file already builds the `.tar.gz` export behind `zurvan snapshot`, and writing the new one there broke the CLI. Found as an ImportError in the full run, original restored (`git diff HEAD -- scripts/snapshot.py` empty), new module renamed `measurement_snapshot.py`. Recorded because the spec proposed the colliding name and a future reader will otherwise repeat it.
- **Five mutants, five caught:** snapshot pointing at the live path, identity re-read from the live index, copy instead of link, release deleting the canonical index, sweep ignoring process liveness.
