- **Verification:** snapshot contracts **14/14**; write attribution **13/13**; guard behaviour **4/4**; `pytest` **947 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed**; `check.sh` **all 24 gates**, with gate 6 reporting `184516 chunks / 4467 sources chunk-set 4b3c1ea33039`; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **A write lease was deliberately NOT built.** Still the right tool for an operation that genuinely needs a stationary store, still the wrong one for an ordinary test run.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) The `--hybrid`-less branch of `eval_search` still globs `wiki/` directly; it is on no gate and is a different retriever, and should be snapshot-fed or deleted. (2) Retrieval bottleneck now measured: the vector scan is **83% of query time** (0.425 s of 0.512 s, full scan over 184,516 vectors) — see the next phase. (3) Frozen baselines gain a snapshot identity going forward, which is what makes "the same measurement" checkable rather than assumed.

### 2026-09-18b (Australia/Sydney) — the gate did not implement the policy that was measured; Boundary v1 is closed
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/boundary_guard.py` (captures and passes the sink baseline), `scripts/boundary/sink.py` (one `git cat-file --batch` for the whole sink), `scripts/eval_boundary_{recall,candidates}.py` (`--via-gate`), `tests/test_boundary_gate_policy.py` (new, 14 contracts), `tests/test_boundary_sink_baseline.py` (+2), `scripts/bench_boundary_gate.py` (gate mode + capture timing), `docs/boundary-v1-closure.md` (new), Boundary spec. **Policy/correctness change, kept separate from the batching commit `dd4410c`.**
- **The measured system and the shipped system had different semantics.** `boundary_guard.main` called `gitscan.scan_staged(root, repo)`, the parameter defaulted to `None`, and scan-side sink provenance — implemented, given 13 contracts, and measured at 0/200 — was **inactive on every real commit**. The 0/200 was evidence about the designed scanner, not about the gate. Reproduced through the real entry point before fixing: a benign edit to an already-published file returned `Boundary: BLOCKED`.
- **Why 13 green contracts missed it.** Every one called `scan_bytes` directly. **A test that reaches past the caller cannot detect a caller that fails to pass the argument.** The new suite enters through `boundary_guard.main`, and one test asserts on the argument itself — at the seam that actually broke rather than below it.
- **`production_commit_gate_verdicts == evaluation_scanner_verdicts` is now asserted**, not assumed. Pinned three ways: a unit test over a mixed staged set, and a `--via-gate` mode on **both** frozen harnesses that re-runs the frozen corpus through `boundary_guard` and exits non-zero on disagreement. Corpora were **not** regenerated, reseeded or tuned — only the execution path was added.
- **Both gates re-measured through the production path.** Operational FP **0/200, guard exit code 0**, same 6 candidates suppressed by sink publicity. Recall **10/10, controls correct, 0 missed**. Recall is the one that had to survive this: a too-generous exemption, or a baseline reader that mis-framed a blob into one giant object, would make every span look already-public, suppress every leak, and make the FP gate look *better*.
- **The baseline is scoped to the repository Boundary PROTECTS, never the one being scanned.** If it followed the scanned repo, `git init && git commit` in a scratch directory would manufacture sink evidence and anyone who can run git could cross the boundary. Captured from allowlisted refs' tip trees only (tree, index, candidate commit, stash, `refs/original/*`, scratch branches all excluded), once per invocation, frozen for the batch, transient, and **fails closed** — no repo or no accepted refs yields an empty baseline granting no exemptions rather than every one.
- **Baseline capture cost, measured separately from the scanner as instructed, and it was unacceptable at first.** One `git cat-file` per object: **0.92 s for 100 sink blobs, 3.16 s for this repository's 399** — a fixed per-commit cost dwarfing the 0.075 s it takes to scan one staged file, which would have erased the batching win. `git cat-file --batch` reads the sink in one process, framed by declared length so blobs containing NULs, CRLF or no trailing newline survive intact (pinned by a byte-exactness test). **3.16 s → 0.075 s, 42×.** The contract asserted is *independence from sink size*, not a magic call count.
- **The 2.6× batching gain survives.** At 100 staged files the gate is **1.552 s** against 1.495 s for the scanner alone (+4%) and 4.299 s pre-batching — **2.8× faster while implementing strictly more policy**. The honest exception is the single-file commit: **0.071 s → 0.156 s**, because a fixed cost has nothing to amortise against at n=1. Peak memory 2.19 → 2.90 MB, the extra being the held sink.
- **A limitation this exposed, pinned rather than hidden.** `d1.confirm` expands maximally, so when appended bytes begin with the byte the protected source continues with — a trailing blank line, ordinary in Markdown — the span reaches past the public blob and a benign edit **still blocks**. Measured: **224-byte span against a 223-byte public blob**. My first test fixture hit this by accident and read as a wiring failure; it is now a deliberate test. Residual subtraction stays unbuilt — and note the operational corpus's edit recipe uses a leading newline and still scored 0/200, so **that corpus does not exercise the straddle either**. Bound stated, not measured away.
- **Verification:** gate-policy contracts **14/14**; sink contracts **15/15**; batching **17/17**; Boundary suites **148/148**; `boundary_redteam` **27/27**; frozen recall **10/10** via scanner **and** via gate; frozen operational FP **0/200** via scanner **and** via gate; `pytest` **916 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed**; `check.sh` **all 24 gates**; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **One `check.sh` run is recorded as INVALIDATED, not failed.** The first attempt halted at gate 2 when the session-scoped guard fired: `decisions: 138 -> 139`, and the appended `wiki/log.md` line named the writer — an Instagram/Meta decision from another project's session at 15:21:04, writing into the shared store mid-gate. Not this code and not these tests, and the re-run passed all 24 gates. Logged here because the standing invariant says an interrupted gate is reported as invalidated by concurrent corpus mutation and never re-run until it happens to pass; one re-run after identifying the external writer is not the same thing as retrying for a green.
- **Boundary v1 is closed.** [docs/boundary-v1-closure.md](docs/boundary-v1-closure.md) records the threat model (careless authorised agent, explicitly **not** hostile), what v1 protects, the provenance and sink-baseline models, recall 10/10, operational FP 0/200, `T = 64` untouched, commit-gate performance before/after, **twelve stated bounds**, and the four numbers that are *not* results (11/200, 1/200, 4/200, 33/200) so none is later mistaken for one.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Adversarial evasion — encoding, fragmentation, substitution, non-text carriers — is a **new design phase with its own threat model**, not an extension of v1, and should start by measuring how far the current detectors fall before any mechanism is chosen. (2) A harder "new file" corpus independent of both sink and protected corpus. (3) Residual subtraction, if evidence ever produces a straddling case. (4) `PUBLIC_DECLARED` promotion path.

### 2026-09-18 (Australia/Sydney) — commit-gate batching: one scan session per commit, 2.6× at 100 staged files, and not one verdict changed
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/boundary/session.py` (new — `ScanSession`, `batch_scan`), `scripts/boundary/scan.py` (`scan_bytes` is now a one-candidate session), `scripts/boundary/gitscan.py` (one session per gate invocation, optional `baseline`), `scripts/boundary/{d2,d3}.py` (load split from decide), `scripts/bench_boundary_gate.py` (new), `tests/test_boundary_batching.py` (new, 17 contracts), Boundary spec. **Performance only:** `T = 64`, the source×sink matrix, D1/D2/D3 semantics, the sink-baseline rule, provenance classes and both frozen corpora are untouched.
- **The old cost model, measured rather than assumed.** The gate called `scan_bytes` once per staged post-image, and each call opened the store, validated it, re-read the config, reloaded every origin and literal row, and rebuilt its blob cache. At 100 staged files on a 150-document fixture: **100 store opens, 100 validations, 100 `stat_digest` tree walks, and 4,056 `store.get_blob` calls** — each of which re-hashes the blob in full, deliberately, so the per-scan cache was throwing away 150 blobs' worth of integrity proof between every file.
- **After: 1, 1, 1, and 150.** Blob reads saturate at the number of protected blobs, so each is read and verified at most once per commit — the floor, not an improvement on it. p50 at 100 files **4.236 s → 1.604 s (2.6×)**; p95 **4.360 s → 1.958 s (2.2×)**; per-file **42.4 ms → 16.0 ms**. At 10 files, 2.1×. **At one staged file there is no speed-up at all (1.0×)** — the work is identical and only its amortisation changed, so the table says so.
- **The session invariant is a security property before it is a performance one.** A `ScanSession` reads protected state, public accessions, the literal registry, the release table and the sink baseline **once, before the first candidate**, and never re-reads them. That is stronger than per-file scanning, not weaker: previously a mid-commit edit to the protected tree raised `StaleStateError` partway down the staged list, so the same staged set got different answers depending on where the edit landed. Staleness now surfaces at the **next** invocation, which still refuses until a rebuild.
- **Intra-batch anti-laundering.** The baseline is frozen and candidates are never added to it. The obvious optimisation — accumulate what you have already scanned — manufactures a laundering primitive that does not otherwise exist: stage the leak twice, the first copy joins the "already public" set, the second reads clean, and one `git add` declassifies protected material. For the same reason match de-duplication stays **per candidate**; hoisting `seen` into the session would drop a real leak from the second file sharing a span with the first.
- **Both invariants were verified by mutation, not by reading.** Each bug was written into `session.py`, the named regression run, the file restored: hoisted `seen`, accumulated baseline, unshared blob cache, per-candidate store open, fail-fast on first block. **Five mutants, five caught.** The mutation ran only against `scripts/boundary/session.py` with every fixture under `tmp_path`; the live store and the real wiki were never in scope.
- **The benchmark found its own first bug.** `scripts/bench_boundary_gate.py` re-staged byte-identical content, so `diff --cached` was empty and it reported 8 ms for 100 files — measuring a gate that scanned nothing. It now asserts the staged path count. It refuses to run against `~/.zurvan/boundary` or the working tree, generates its corpus from fixed seeds, and runs both modes over **one** fixture so before/after is a property of the code path rather than of two corpora.
- **Stated cost.** Peak traced memory at 100 files rises **1.29 MB → 2.19 MB**. The shared blob cache is bounded by the protected corpus, not the candidate count — 150 blobs at 50 files and at 100 — so on the real 11.2 MB corpus the ceiling is roughly that, once per commit.
- **Correctness: nothing moved.** Blocked counts identical across pre-change isolated, post-change isolated and post-change batch at every size (1/1, 2/10, 3/50, 3/100). Batching **17/17**; all Boundary suites **95/95**; `boundary_redteam` **27/27**; frozen recall **10/10** with controls correct and zero leaks suppressed; frozen operational FP **0/200** with the same **6** candidates suppressed by sink publicity; `pytest` **900 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed**; `check.sh` 23 gates; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **An open item this exposed and did NOT fix.** The commit gate never passes a baseline — `boundary_guard.main` → `gitscan.scan_staged` supplies `baseline=None` — so scan-side sink provenance, which is implemented, tested and measured at 0/200, **is not active in the real gate**. `scan_staged` now takes a `baseline` parameter so wiring it is one line at the caller, but that would change live verdicts and is a policy decision, not a performance one.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Decide whether the commit gate should capture and pass a sink baseline. (2) Structural read-only for the session connection (`mode=ro`) would make "the gate mutates nothing" an invariant of the handle rather than of a byte-identity test. (3) Adversarial evasion — encoding, fragmentation, substitution, transformed leaks — remains explicitly out of scope for v1.

### 2026-09-17d (Australia/Sydney) — both Boundary gates measured against frozen corpora: recall 10/10, operational false positives 0/200, T untouched
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/eval_boundary_recall.py` (new), `scripts/eval_boundary_candidates.py` (new), `eval/boundary_recall_v1.manifest.json`, `eval/boundary_fp_operational_v1.manifest.json`, Boundary spec. Frozen corpora live in `~/.zurvan/instruments/boundary-{recall-v1,fp-operational-v1}/`, never in git.
- **Recall — the question that had been NOT MEASURED since Boundary shipped — is 10/10, 100%.** Eleven fixtures: long prose, exactly `T`, `T−1` as a control (correctly CLEAN), embedded in benign text, multiple separated spans, code/config, prose, and four built specifically to attack match-level sink provenance — public bytes before the private span, after it, on **both sides**, and a private span appended to a wholly public file. All four block. **Zero leaks were suppressed by the sink baseline**, which is what makes the FP number below safe to accept.
- **Operational false positives: 0/200 — 0/100 modified, 0/100 new.** The decisive figure is not the zero: **six candidates block WITHOUT the sink baseline and are clean with it**. Six exceeds the ceiling of one, so without scan-side sink provenance this gate fails. That is the measurement showing the rule does real work rather than merely reading well.
- **I rejected my own first operational run, at 33/200.** Its "new" half was sourced from `raw/`, the same tree as the protected corpus — which held **254 documents from one export family** — so all 100 candidates were siblings of protected documents, genuinely shared content with them, and were largely blocked **correctly**. Recorded as **invalid by construction, not a detector failure**. The corrected half comes from the repository's own git history: `raw/` has never been committed, so nothing there can have an ingestion relationship with the protected corpus.
- **Stated bound on the 0/200.** The "new" half is superseded versions of tracked files — real candidate blobs a gate sees on a revert or cherry-pick, but largely sink-public and therefore the easier case. A corpus of genuinely novel documents unrelated to both the sink and the protected corpus would be harder and is not available locally.
- **`T = 64` is unchanged and no detector policy was tuned.** Three explanations for the earlier failures were measured and rejected before any change: byte entropy (**inverted** — 4.74/4.21 bits per byte for the false positives against **4.02** for genuine protected prose), protected-origin frequency (1 of 300 origins), and the threshold itself.
- **Residual subtraction deliberately NOT implemented.** The straddling-span limitation is real and pinned by a test, but neither frozen corpus produced a case — 0 false positives, 0 suppressed leaks. Building it now would be designing against a predicted failure rather than an observed one.
- **Verification:** recall **10/10** with controls correct; operational FP **0/200**; sink contracts **13/13**; harness contracts **11/11**; provenance **37/37**; `boundary_redteam` **27/27**; `pytest` **870 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed** (uncontaminated run); `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) A harder "new file" corpus — genuinely novel documents independent of both the sink and the protected corpus — would strengthen the 0/200. (2) Residual subtraction, if evidence ever produces a straddling case. (3) Commit-gate batching, now unblocked: both gates have valid results. (4) `PUBLIC_DECLARED` still has no promotion path for an audit that verifies a declared URL.

### 2026-09-17b (Australia/Sydney) — provenance at ingestion: three weaker definitions of "public" were implemented, demonstrated exploitable, and rejected
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/provenance.py` (new), `scripts/ingest.py` (frontmatter + accession + `--public-url`), `tests/test_ingest_provenance.py` (new, 37 contracts), Boundary spec §8.
- **The open item that blocked the FP gate is implemented.** `ingest.py` recorded `title`, `hash`, `figures`, `extraction_status` and `ingested_at` — nothing about origin — so a document's class came from the directory it landed in. Everything under `wiki/sources/` was PRIVATE, including this repository's own README, and Boundary then correctly refused to let the original public file be committed. A source page now carries `origin_class` and `origin_provenance`, decided when the document is acceded.
- **Three classes, because "public" is not one thing.** `PRIVATE -> PUBLIC_DECLARED -> PUBLIC_VERIFIED`. Only `PUBLIC_VERIFIED` is acceded to D3, so only it can exempt a span; `PUBLIC_DECLARED` is recorded for audit and never honoured, because an unverified claim that grants an exemption *is* the laundering primitive.
- **Three weaker predicates were written, shown to be exploitable, and rejected.** (1) `git cat-file -e` proves an object EXISTS — `git hash-object -w` satisfies it with no commit, ref or intent; measured on a real repository, the dangling blob reports EXISTS while `git log --find-object` correctly finds nothing. Anyone able to write a file could have manufactured public provenance. (2) `git log --all` means every ref under `refs/`, including `refs/stash`, `refs/original/*` and scratch branches; measured, a stashed file and a temp-branch file were both found, so `git stash` would have laundered a private document. (3) The index is not a sink: `git add` is local, unshared and reversible, so staging a document to have it acceded and then unstaging it would leave the text exempt forever.
- **`PUBLIC_VERIFIED` = reachable from an ALLOWLISTED ref in the repository this gate protects.** Default allowlist is the canonical branch as git reports it (`origin/HEAD`, else `main`/`master`); a repository with neither verifies nothing. `ZURVAN_PROTECTED_REFS` declares release refs. A **foreign repository** classes DECLARED: `git init && git add && git commit` in a scratch directory is not a publication event, and treating it as one would mean anyone who can run git can cross the boundary. One existing test was **inverted deliberately** — a side-branch commit no longer verifies until it is merged.
- **An accession is historical evidence** and survives a later reset, rebase or branch deletion: the disclosure already happened, re-validating would make a routine rebase silently re-block clean content, and revocation belongs in the declassification ledger rather than in `git rebase`. Stated cost: bytes acceded from a rewritten commit stay exempt until revoked — which is why each accession records the blob OID and a repository identity (its root-commit OID; a filesystem path would name a private project inside a stored page).
- **FP gate status — unchanged, deliberately.** Mechanism **IMPLEMENTED and experimentally validated**: an ingestion round-trip blocks without provenance (reproducing the 2026-09-16 class), clears with it, and private material still blocks. Legacy corpus **INVALID for the v2 criterion** — none of the ~4,400 existing pages carries a class, and Global Constraint 7 forbids upgrading them retroactively. The prior **11/200 remains historically valid** for the provenance-blind setup it measured. The v2 FP gate is **NOT YET MEASURED**: a qualifying post-provenance corpus does not exist yet.
- **The v2 gate was then measured, and it FAILED: 4/200, 3/199 independent.** The harness (`scripts/eval_boundary_fp.py`) keeps the provenance seed corpus and the benign scan corpus apart and refuses to run if they touch; artefacts are counted, not anchors. A 40-document pilot returned 1/200 PASS and is recorded as **underpowered** rather than erased — the same frozen 200 give 4/200 against a 300-document protected corpus. Digest disjointness proved insufficient (a fixture sits verbatim inside `scripts/e2e_smoke.sh`), so containment is detected and reported separately.
- **Characterisation rejected both obvious fixes before any tuning.** Byte entropy is **inverted** — the surviving false positives scored 4.74 and 4.21 bits/byte against **4.02** for genuine protected prose. Protected-origin frequency never fires — each span was in **1 of 300** origins. Every remaining match was a span **already committed to this repository's canonical branch**, so the diagnosis is missing scan-side provenance, not a wrong `T`. **`T = 64` is unchanged.**
- **Scan-side sink provenance implemented** (`scripts/boundary/sink.py`, 13 contracts): a matched span is not a NEW disclosure when those exact bytes were in the accepted sink **before the candidate operation began**. Match-level, never file-level; the candidate can never prove itself; restricted to `PRIVATE -> PUBLIC_ARTIFACT`. The baseline is **transient and never acceded**, which is how it satisfies Global Constraint 7 rather than violating it — remove the text from the canonical branch and the exemption goes with it. Bounds stated and tested: tip trees only (deleted content stops being evidence, unlike an accession), and a span straddling the public/new boundary is not exempt, so a benign reformat can still block.
- **Attempt A is preserved and is NOT the operational gate.** All 200 of its artefacts were already-committed blobs, which does not model candidate commit blobs; it is kept as a regression corpus for sink-publicity behaviour.
- **Verification:** provenance contracts **37/37**; `boundary_redteam` **27/27**; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean; full `pytest` recorded below. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Build the post-provenance FP evaluation corpus the v2 criterion requires — not by re-running the legacy one. (2) Commit-gate batching stays blocked until that measurement is valid. (3) `PUBLIC_DECLARED` has no promotion path yet: an audit that verifies a declared URL cannot record that finding.

### 2026-09-17 (Australia/Sydney) — the index had two ways to die and no way to say so; one of the three failures that day was mine
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/rebuild_search_index.py` (rebuilt around a temp build + atomic swap), `scripts/index_health.py` (new), `scripts/agent_workflow.py`, `scripts/cli.py`, `scripts/check.sh`, `scripts/eval_search.py`, `scripts/hybrid_search.py`, `scripts/e2e_all_phases_until_17.sh`, `.claude/settings.json`, `.gitignore`, `tests/test_index_integrity.py` (new, 31 contracts), `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_agent_workflow.py`.
- **Found:** the canonical index was unusable in two ways at once. `data/search.sqlite` held **2,000 of 184,231 chunks** from **59 of 4,418 sources** — a SessionStart hook started a rebuild at 13:23:49 and the session exited at 13:24:32, so SIGKILL left exactly one `COMMIT_EVERY` batch. `data/search.sqlite.prev` held all 184,231 chunks with **every vector `mock`/`deterministic_hash`**, written in 38 s by a rebuild in a shell where `ZURVAN_EMBED_PROVIDER` was unset. Both passed every check the project had; counts agreed in both.
- **One sentence in two places.** The rebuild moved the live index to `.prev` and built AT the canonical path, so any interruption published a partial index. And `get_embedding` returns hashes as a successful result when the provider is unset **or when SentenceTransformers fails to load**. `check.sh` gate 5 rebuilds production and pinned nothing — the likeliest source of the poisoning.
- **Atomic rebuild invariant.** Build at `data/search.sqlite.building-<pid>`, validate there, promote with one `os.replace()`; the old index stays readable (`mode=ro`, in place) for embedding reuse throughout. Exception, SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGKILL or failed validation all leave the canonical path holding the old index. `.prev` is gone and carries no correctness weight. A build file owned by a live process makes the next rebuild refuse to start.
- **Exact-provider invariant.** Abort when the provider received ≠ requested (the silent fallback), and abort a CANONICAL rebuild unless the space is exactly `sentence_transformers`/`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`/`384`. `zurvan index search` carries the production space itself instead of inheriting the shell.
- **Health is not a row count.** `index_health.py` checks integrity, chunk/FTS/embedding agreement, source coverage, a single embedding space, that space being expected, and every vector's byte width. Exposed as `zurvan index health [--deep]`, enforced in gate 5, reported on the session-start card. Asked with no stated expectation, a mock index is UNHEALTHY.
- **Test isolation, and my own failure.** Mutation-testing the new guards I removed both; this file's "a canonical rebuild is refused" test then rebuilt the production index and deleted it. The 2,000-chunk partial was already condemned, but a guard whose own test can destroy what it guards has one point of failure. Canonical-path tests now run against a disposable root; no canonical rebuild can start while `PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST` is set; `conftest.py` fails the session if `data/search.sqlite` changes at all; an AST meta-test rejects any test starting a rebuild without naming a disposable target.
- **Read paths were creating the index.** `agent_prime` left a 0-byte database at the canonical path. All ten readers now open `mode=ro`; exactly one write-mode connection remains and it opens the build path.
- **Two hazards found by accident.** A backtick in a shell audit command executed `zurvan index search` and ran a second production rebuild for ten minutes beside the first — atomicity meant neither corrupted anything, but nothing refused it. And renaming `.prev` to `.building-<pid>` escaped `.gitignore`'s `data/*.sqlite`, leaving a 737 MB index of private knowledge one `git add -A` from staging; rules now match the shape, with a test over every artefact name.
- **Recovery:** one foreground rebuild, provider pinned, not hook-driven — **184,241 chunks = 184,241 embeddings = 184,241 FTS rows, 4,421 of 4,421 indexable sources**, one space, 0 mock, 0 wrong-width, `integrity_check` ok, no leftovers. The **+10 chunks / +3 sources** against the old index are three pages written after it was built (6+2+2 chunks); **zero sources removed**.
- **Retrieval:** Top-1 0.70 → **0.72**, Top-3 0.74 → **0.76**, MRR 0.7297 → **0.7457**, failures 13 → **12**, against the frozen 2026-09-15 baseline. **49/50 queries hold their exact rank; zero regressions.** The single mover is item #47 (rank 5 → 1), attributable to the `CLAUDE.md` rewrite committed in `03445e7` AFTER that baseline was frozen — **no causal retrieval gain is claimed for this recovery**, which restored behaviour rather than improving it. No gold query appears verbatim in any indexed root file.
- **Verification:** `pytest` **822 passed, 4 skipped, 0 failed**; `data/search.sqlite` byte-identical (`f739ab6c…`) before and after the suite; index integrity **31/31**; `boundary_redteam` **27/27**; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. **`check.sh` could not complete and is reported as INVALIDATED, not passed:** twice it aborted at gate 2 when a session in another project wrote a note into the shared wiki through MCP (14:57:12 and 15:03:52). The guard is correct — the corpus moved mid-measurement — and was not weakened; its message now prints the `wiki/log.md` lines appended during the run so the writer is identifiable at a glance. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) **Measurement snapshots** — `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-17-measurement-snapshot-design.md`; gates must read a hard-linked snapshot of the index rather than assume a shared store holds still, with one open question (whether an attributed external write should still fail the suite) left for Raouf. (2) Gates 3–23 of `check.sh` have not run since these changes. (3) `zurvan index search` from an unpinned shell now REFUSES rather than poisoning — correct, but it is a behaviour change for anyone with a script that calls it.

### 2026-09-16d (Australia/Sydney) — the first measurement against real material broke the FP gate, and the cause was not the detector
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/boundary/{d1,scan,store}.py`, `scripts/boundary_redteam.py` (+section F), `scripts/check.sh` (concurrency guard + named gate-0 suites), `tests/test_boundary_gauntlet.py` (rows 18-21), `tests/test_boundary_store.py`, Boundary spec, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/agents-history.md`.
- **Correcting 2026-09-16c.** Its "scan p95 **379 ms**" was `validate()` alone presented as end-to-end. The real full-scan result on the same corpus was **p95 34,501 ms / p50 14,957 ms**, with **FP 32/200** against a ≤1/200 ceiling.
- **One defect produced both numbers.** A tracked file yielded **63,975 candidate anchors** from a horizontal rule (`─` × 90) it shared with a protected page: D1 treated 270 bytes of a three-byte unit as 270 bytes of protected content. T now counts **informative length**; degenerate k-grams are not fingerprinted on either side. Anchors **63,975 → 809**, p95 **34,501 ms → 344 ms** (budget 400).
- **Both directions pinned** (gauntlet rows 18-20): padding cannot manufacture a match, padding cannot conceal one, and prose buried between two long runs stays reachable. Row 17 could not fail — its clean corpus shared no vocabulary with the protected doc — so row 21 rebuilds it from the protected document's own words.
- **FP recorded as invalid as measured** (see standing invariants). All 11 survivors read; none is a coincidence. Provenance-at-ingestion is a **design-gated open item, not implemented here**.
- **`D1_VERSION` → `d1-winnow-v2-informative`**, so pre-change stores and releases fail closed. A 73 ms optimisation (orphan join → `deep=True`) was found and **refused**: the budget already passes, and loosening a live integrity check to improve a passing number is not done here.
- **Index corruption caused and repaired.** A concurrent `check.sh` + rebuild left the live index at **8,000 of 185,303 chunks** — valid-looking, 96% missing. Rebuilt to **184,198**, disk == index == embeddings, VERDICT faithful. `check.sh` now refuses to start while a rebuild is in flight.
- **Verification:** `pytest` **792 passed, 4 skipped, 0 failed**; `boundary_redteam` **27/27**; `check.sh` **23/23, exit 0**; `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Docs-accuracy pass — `CLAUDE.md` was telling agents something false.** It described `wiki/` as *tracked*; since 2026-09-15c only 11 `.gitkeep` markers are, and `.gitignore:94` (`wiki/**`) keeps every page out by default. It also pointed at `eval/` for the retrieval gold set, which moved to `~/.zurvan/instruments/`, and documented none of Boundary. Now carries the verified `boundary` subcommands, the rebuild-concurrency rule, the never-quote-the-instrument rule, and the failed-gate rule. Every claim in it was checked by running the thing it describes. `2026-09-15c` moved **verbatim** to `docs/agents-history.md` (diff-confirmed identical) to restore the two-entry limit.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) provenance at ingestion — blocks the FP gate; (2) the commit gate pays ~200 ms revalidation **per staged blob** (~10 s for a 50-file commit); a batch scan session would remove it at the cost of a TOCTOU window — design change, not an optimisation; (3) the 7-leak recall corpus still does not exist, so recall is **NOT MEASURED**; (4) a 400-byte true positive still reports one match per anchor.

### 2026-09-16c (Australia/Sydney) — Boundary Plans 2 and 3 shipped, then red-teamed: four instrument leaks found in this repository, including one in the report that states the rule
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/instruments.py` (new), `scripts/boundary/gitscan.py` (new), `scripts/boundary_guard.py` (new), `scripts/boundary_redteam.py` (new), `scripts/check.sh` (gate 0), `scripts/cli.py` (+`boundary` subcommands), `scripts/boundary/{store,d1,d3,scan}.py`, plus `scripts/chunk.py`, `scripts/e2e_smoke.sh` and two frozen retrieval reports whose contents leaked the instrument. Tests: `test_instruments.py`, `test_boundary_gitscan.py`, `test_boundary_guard.py` (new).
- **Plan 3 — the instrument left git.** `eval/search_gold.jsonl` was tracked while the policy declares `INSTRUMENT -> PUBLIC_ARTIFACT` blocked, so the scanner could not be enabled without rejecting its own repository. Cleartext now lives in `~/.zurvan/instruments/`; git keeps `eval/search_gold.manifest.json` with a count and one whole-file digest. **No per-item digest**, deliberately: hashing a ~50-byte natural-language item individually is a confirmation oracle for anyone holding a candidate.
- **Plan 2 — the commit gate.** `gitscan` reads the COMPLETE staged post-image of every changed path, never diff hunks, because a protected span can be assembled across two hunks or moved by a rename with no textual diff. Sink labels are assigned per path and are non-exclusive, so `docs/x.md` is both `PUBLIC_ARTIFACT` and `RETRIEVAL_CORPUS` and deny-overrides-allow actually fires. The gate is **opt-in**: with no store it prints NOT CONFIGURED and exits 0, because a gate that blocks every commit before setup gets bypassed and then removed.
- **Three measured performance defects, each found by profiling rather than guessing.** (1) Winnowing was O(n·w) — a 300-page build exceeded four minutes and was killed; the monotonic-deque rewrite is O(n) and does it in **7.8 s**, with a property test pinning its output to the naive definition because a faster winnow that picks different minima invalidates every stored fingerprint. (2) `validate()` ran one `COUNT(*)` per origin — 301 full scans of 796,703 rows, **~17 s per scan, flat regardless of input**. My first hypothesis (manifest re-hashing) was wrong and I fixed the wrong thing before profiling; the real cause was a missing index on `fingerprints.origin_id`. (3) The `GROUP BY` replacement still cost ~400 ms per scan, so the per-scan check became two cheap aggregates and the per-origin breakdown moved to `deep=True`. **17 s -> 379 ms against a 400 ms budget.**
- **That optimisation introduced a fail-open path, and the gauntlet caught it.** Comparing only `count(fingerprints)` let `UPDATE origins SET n_fingerprints=...` pass, because no fingerprint row changed. Validation now compares **both** the row count and `sum(origins.n_fingerprints)` against the committed total. A separate fail-open — treating an absent `total_fingerprints` as "nothing to check" — would have made `DELETE FROM meta WHERE key='total_fingerprints'` a one-row bypass of the entire guarantee; it now raises, with its own regression test.
- **The red-team found four instrument leaks in this repository.** `scripts/chunk.py` quoted gold item #44 **inside the comment explaining why evaluation reports are excluded**; `scripts/e2e_smoke.sh` used a gold item as a live search probe; and two frozen retrieval reports listed failing items verbatim — including `sqlite-vec-2026-09-15.md`, **the report that states the rule**, which listed all 13. All now reference items by INDEX. One June provenance trace also contains gold item #1, and is a **frozen artifact that must not be mutated**, so it is named as an explicit, auditable exception rather than silently skipped.
- **Files Changed:** as above, plus `AGENTS.md` + `CHANGELOG.md`. `scripts/boundary_redteam.py` is wired into `check.sh` gate 0 — a gate nobody attacks is a gate nobody has tested.
- **Verification:** `pytest` -> **784 passed, 4 skipped, 0 failed**. `boundary_redteam` -> **24/24** (3 block/pass checks, 3 output-leak checks, 11 integrity attacks, 3 policy fail-open probes, 4 git-exposure checks). `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. Real-corpus measurement on 300 real source pages (13 MB) + the live instrument: build 7.8 s, 796,703 fingerprints, scan p95 **379 ms**. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) The 379 ms still carries a ~315 ms fixed cost independent of input size; headroom against the 400 ms budget is thin and a larger corpus will breach it. (2) A total-preserving delete-and-insert passes the per-scan check and is caught only by `deep=True` — a hostile agent, explicitly out of v1 scope, stated rather than discovered. (3) The stat probe assumes an edit changes size or mtime; same scope boundary. (4) A 400-byte true positive produces 252 matches, one per fingerprint anchor — correct but noisy, and worth collapsing per span before anyone reads a report.

### 2026-09-15c (Australia/Sydney) — The knowledge base leaves git entirely: code is tracked, data is not
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `.gitignore` and the tracking status of every file under `wiki/`. No code behaviour changes.
- **Per Raouf (2026-09-15): "all data has to private and not goes anywhere".** The Zurvan CODE is what lives in git; the data inside Zurvan does not. **68 tracked files under `wiki/` were untracked** — 22 decisions, 25 traces, 8 templates, 7 summaries, 5 concepts, 1 digest, plus `overview.md`, `open-questions.md` and three notes. They remain on disk and in the local store; only git tracking is removed.
- **The rule is now a blanket, not a list.** `.gitignore` already blocked `wiki/sources/`, `wiki/claims/`, `wiki/index.md`, `wiki/log.md` and `note-*.md`, each added after a specific leak — the file's own comments record that a `.md.pre-repair.bak` and case-sensitive globs had both defeated earlier rules. A per-directory list makes a NEW directory public by default until someone remembers it. `wiki/**` (with `!wiki/**/` and `!wiki/**/.gitkeep`) inverts that: private by default. Verified by probe — a fresh page written into `wiki/decisions/` and `wiki/concepts/` is ignored by rule, `git check-ignore` confirming `.gitignore:94:wiki/**` for both.
- **What stays tracked, and why it is not private data.** `data/extractions/` is synthetic fixtures (`claim-dummy-001`, "A short summary"). `data/traces/` and `eval/*.jsonl` are the frozen research instruments, referencing `docs/` paths rather than ingested content, and are protected by the standing frozen-artifact rule. `.gitkeep` markers stay so a fresh clone still has the directory skeleton the writers need — they do not create their own parents.
- **Consequence worth knowing:** a fresh clone now has code and an empty knowledge base. That is the intent, but it means the `wiki/`-dependent smokes and the 50-query eval are meaningful only against a local store. `public_repo_guard` keeps running as defence in depth even though it now has nothing under `wiki/` to gate.
- **Files Changed:** `.gitignore` (+blanket `wiki/**` rule), 68 files untracked under `wiki/`, 11 `.gitkeep` markers added, AGENTS.md + CHANGELOG.md.
- **Verification:** `pytest` -> **698 passed, 0 failed**. `public_repo_guard` passed (exit 0); `git diff --check` clean. Probe confirmed new `wiki/` pages are unstageable. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) An earlier commit this session (`dd97624`) had tracked four decision pages; `eccb360` untracked them, and this entry removes the rest. (2) Anything that needs to travel between machines now has to move outside git — worth deciding how before a second machine is involved.

### 2026-09-15b (Australia/Sydney) — The tests were writing into the corpus the tests measure, and so was this session's own write-back
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/memory.py` (project attribution), `scripts/config.py` + `scripts/safe_write.py` (`ZURVAN_ROOT`), `scripts/session.py` + `scripts/agent_workflow.py` (store-root / code-root split), `scripts/check.sh` (gate 11), `scripts/cli.py` + `scripts/mcp_tools.py` (`--project`), `tests/conftest.py` (new guard), four test files, and two frozen artifacts under `docs/evaluation/retrieval/`. Corpus change, so the before/after eval is recorded below per invariant 4. A hygiene cycle run BEFORE Phase 1B-FTS, because both defects contaminate the benchmark that phase optimises against.
- **1. A decision could inherit a project it had nothing to do with.** `current_project_name()` returned the registry's `current` unconditionally, so four decisions written from inside this repo were stamped with an unrelated project — a workspace left selected by an unrelated session. `public_repo_guard` gates publication on that field, so this is a publication control, not a label. Now `resolve_project()` resolves, fail-closed: explicit `--project`; else the registered project CONTAINING the working directory (deepest wins, so write-back from any repo still attributes correctly); else the registry's `current` only when it agrees anyway; else `unknown`, which is not in `PUBLIC_PROJECTS` and is therefore blocked rather than published under a guess. Five regression tests pin it, including the exact failing case. `--project` is wired through the CLI and the MCP tool. The ambiguous privacy-denylist decision was left untouched — uncertain metadata is not corrected by guessing.
- **2. Six writers were putting test and session output into the live corpus.** `tests/test_cli.py::test_cli_remember` (48 notes, its comment read "will write to wiki but that's safe in test"); the MCP smoke's write-mode session (28 notes, 4 decisions, 7 duplicate claims); `check.sh` gate 11 (6 session pages); `tests/test_mcp_tools.py::test_tool_zurvan_remember` (187 log appends); `tests/test_wiki_merge.py` (17 log appends per run); and this session's own write-back (see 4). All of it indexed — so test execution was moving the chunk count, the retrieval candidates, the graph and every baseline the project measures against.
- **3. Four of the six were partial isolation that LOOKED complete.** A patched store root with an unpatched log. A page redirected by `wiki_dir=` while `append_log_merge` wrote through a module-level `PROJECT_ROOT`. A write paired with an `os.remove` — file counts balanced, log appends permanent, and one crash from leaving the page behind. And an exclusion rule covering a directory but not verbatim quotation. **That pattern is the finding**; the individual fixes matter less than distrusting isolation that has not been asserted. Every fix now asserts it: the smoke fails if it wrote nothing to its throwaway root OR if anything appeared live.
- **4. This session's own write-back contaminated the evaluation, which is the sharpest lesson here.** The first Phase 1B baseline measured Top-1 **0.68** against Phase 1's 0.70. The mover was not BM25 drift: `wiki/decisions/exclude-docsevaluationretrieval-from-indexing--but.md` — a decision written this session ABOUT preventing evaluation contamination — quoted a gold query verbatim in its reason and outranked that query's real answer. The same quote had gone into the `AGENTS.md` entry, which is also indexed. Measured repair: real answer rank 3 -> (quote removed from the decision) rank 2 -> (removed from AGENTS.md too) **rank 1**, Top-1 back to **0.70**. Exclusion cannot fix this class, because decisions and changelog entries are legitimately indexed knowledge. The rule is editorial and now stated in both artifacts: **refer to gold queries by number; never reproduce their text in an indexed page.**
- **5. The guard that found two of these had a blind spot I built into it.** `tests/conftest.py` fails the session if the live store moves. Its first version deliberately ignored `wiki/log.md`, reasoning that the log is append-only and changes legitimately — true in general, false during a test run, where nothing should append. That exemption is exactly what hid the 187-append and 17-append writers. It now measures log LENGTH too. **The check that never missed anything was re-deriving the corpus from disk and diffing it against the index** — it found gate 11 in a run a count-based guard had passed clean, and it belongs in `check.sh` as a gate.
- **Files Changed:** `scripts/memory.py` (+`resolve_project`, `add_decision(project=)`), `scripts/config.py` + `scripts/safe_write.py` (`ZURVAN_ROOT`), `scripts/session.py` + `scripts/agent_workflow.py` (`CODE_ROOT` vs store `ROOT`), `scripts/check.sh` (gate 11 isolated + leak assertions), `scripts/cli.py` (`--project`), `scripts/mcp_tools.py` (`project` param), `tests/conftest.py` (new), `tests/test_memory.py` (+5), `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_mcp_tools.py`, `tests/test_wiki_merge.py` (8 sites), `tests/test_session.py` + `tests/test_agent_workflow.py` (CODE_ROOT), `docs/evaluation/retrieval/phase-1b-baseline-2026-09-15.md` + `...-ranks-2026-09-15.json` (new), AGENTS.md + CHANGELOG.md + docs/agents-history.md. Removed: 48 test notes, 28 smoke notes, 4 smoke decisions, 7 duplicate smoke claims, 6 session pages, 8 smoke question blocks (the unsuffixed question survived — it is genuine and was resolved with a real decision).
- **Verification:** `pytest` -> **698 passed, 0 failed, 0 errors** with the guard active. `check.sh` -> **all 23 gates passed** AND measured corpus-inert: wiki 97 / decisions 107 / claims 248 / sessions 0 / sources 3822 / log 11,534 lines, identical before and after the run. `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. `eval_search --hybrid` before (184,171, Phase 1) **0.70 / 0.74 / 0.7297 / 13 failures**; after (184,002) **0.70 / 0.74 / 0.73 / 13 failures** — same figures after removing ~90 artifact pages, which is the evidence the cycle was corrective rather than disruptive. Index verified a faithful image of disk: 184,002 chunks re-derived by the chunker, identical IDs, zero orphans either direction, real (non-mock) vectors. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) **Ten modules still derive their own root from `__file__`** — only the two that write into `wiki/` were fixed; any new writer copying the pattern reopens the hole. (2) **Put the disk-vs-index diff in `check.sh`** as a gate; it is the only check here that cannot have a blind spot. (3) The 67 duplicate pages remain queued as their own gated cleanup. (4) `wiki/log.md` keeps 187 historical `Note Added - MCP test` entries and the older merge appends — inert now that the writers are fixed, and rewriting them would edit the log's own record. (5) Phase 1B-FTS is now unblocked and measures against **184,002 · 0.70 / 0.74 / 0.73 · 13 failures**, with per-query ranks frozen by gold query NUMBER so the next comparison can name its movers instead of inferring them.

### 2026-09-15 (Australia/Sydney) — Phase 1 `sqlite-vec`: correctness PASS, performance gate FAIL, and the bottleneck turned out to be the keyword side
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/vec_storage.py` (new), `scripts/hybrid_search.py` (+`_Ranked`), `scripts/rebuild_search_index.py` (BLOB vectors, JSON->BLOB reuse path), `scripts/chunk.py` (+`EXCLUDED_PATH_PREFIXES`), `requirements.txt` (pin), the frozen report `docs/evaluation/retrieval/sqlite-vec-2026-09-15.md`, and tests. Ranking/indexing change, so before/after `eval_search` is recorded below per invariant 4. **The two existing retrieval reports moved** to `docs/evaluation/retrieval/` (paths cited in the 2026-09-14 and 14d entries have changed accordingly).
- **Decision: GATE A NOT ACCEPTED.** The storage swap is ranking-neutral, ~4x faster and 3.3x smaller on disk, and it is kept — but the pre-registered p50/p95 thresholds were not met and the gate is NOT being redefined to say otherwise. Correctness: top-10 ordering **50/50 identical**, **0** real semantic inversions (score gap > 1e-6), max delta **3.398e-07** against a 1e-6 bound, **0.70 / 0.74 / 0.7297** and the **same 13 failing query IDs**. Performance: p50 **701.8 ms** (gate < 300), p95 **2531.6 ms** (gate < 500).
- **The gate contained a contradiction, and finding it was the main result.** Gate A demanded ordering identical to the reference AND a deterministic `chunk_id` tie-break. Both cannot hold: the reference had no tie rule. The first run measured 48/50, and neither difference was a rerank — both were byte-identical chunks from duplicate wiki pages (`wiki/sources/medium_article.md` vs `medium_article.md.md`, and a Codex article under two slugs) with identical vectors and exactly equal scores. Equal scores were settled by whichever row the scan reached first, because the heap displaced only on `hybrid > heap[0][0]` and `_fuse`'s stable sort inherited `_hydrate`'s SQLite fetch order. **An implementation accident was standing in for a ranking rule**, and Phase 1 only changed the accident. The contract is corrected prospectively — unequal scores must match the reference; exact ties obey one canonical rule, `score DESC, chunk_id ASC` — and applied to BOTH paths via `_Ranked`, a single ordering definition the heap and `_fuse` defer to. `chunk_id` is compared as an opaque string, with a test, so no hash/hex/width assumption leaks into ranking. Under the corrected contract: **50/50**.
- **`docs/evaluation/` was feeding the retriever its own lab notebook.** Evaluation reports are documents ABOUT measured retrieval behaviour, and indexing them closes a loop: the Step 0 report added 7 chunks to the index whose baseline it froze, and surfaced again inside an unexplained +28 chunk drift during this phase's reconciliation. Same defect `wiki/traces/` was excluded for. **But excluding all of `docs/evaluation/` was measured and reverted**: it removed `docs/evaluation/provenance.md`, the sole expected answer for one of the 50 gold queries (#44, on how replayed traces are graded — gold queries are the measuring instrument and are referenced by number here, never reproduced verbatim, because an indexed page that quotes one outranks that query's real answer), dropping Top-3 0.74 -> 0.72 and failures 13 -> 14 — a self-inflicted `index_coverage_miss`, the exact class Step 0 was built to detect. Only reports about RETRIEVAL close the loop, so the rule is `docs/evaluation/retrieval/` and the reports were moved there. The new report excludes itself.
- **Removing the global `ORDER BY` was ranking-neutral, and proved to be more than a speed change.** `scan_similarities` sorted every row (`ORDER BY d ASC, chunk_id ASC`), measured at 2.92 s against 1.37 s without it — roughly half the query cost buying an ordering the bounded heap discards. It was also the only thing making tied results deterministic, i.e. a ranking rule living inside a storage function. With the rule moved to `_Ranked`, the sort came out and invariance was re-run: **identical in every figure**. The test that asserted ordering at the storage layer was rewritten to assert it at the ranking layer — it now feeds every permutation of scan order in and requires the same winner.
- **The headline finding: `sqlite-vec` was not the remaining latency problem.** Staged profiling gives query embedding **17 ms**, FTS keyword **309 ms**, semantic scan + Python fusion **392 ms**. The keyword side matches a mean of **118,348 rows per query** (max 158,598 of 184,171) because the query is `"t1" OR "t2" OR ...`, so common words sweep most of the corpus. Even a perfect native fusion leaves p50 near **326 ms**, still over the gate. **Corpus-wide candidate generation is the bottleneck, not vector storage** — which redirects Phase 1B to bounded LEXICAL candidates before semantic KNN, and defers native SQL fusion entirely (bounded candidates may make the Python loop cheap enough that it never earns its complexity).
- **The RSS criterion is an invalid attribution metric, not a pass.** RSS is 22 MB at import, **866 MB after the sentence-transformers model loads, and 866 MB after 50 searches** — retrieval adds no measurable memory. `< 400 MB` process RSS is unreachable in-process whatever storage does, and the retired 894 MB figure measured the same model. Recorded as INVALID rather than reinterpreted as a pass. Its replacement, for future phases only, measures retrieval-path delta RSS against a model-resident baseline.
- **Corpus arithmetic, reconciled.** `184,185` before -> `184,105` (blanket exclusion, -80) -> `184,171` (narrowed, -14 from 184,185; the 66 provenance chunks returned). The index was verified a faithful image of disk at each step — chunk IDs re-derived independently by the chunker, identical sets, every chunk embedded, zero orphans either direction. This check, not the arithmetic, is what closed the original +28 question: it proved the new storage path indexes nothing differently.
- **Files Changed:** `scripts/vec_storage.py` (new), `scripts/hybrid_search.py` (+`_Ranked`, `_score_semantic_top_k`, `_hydrate`, `_fuse`), `scripts/rebuild_search_index.py`, `scripts/chunk.py` (+`EXCLUDED_PATH_PREFIXES`), `requirements.txt` (`sqlite-vec==0.1.6`), `tests/test_vec_storage.py` (new, 11), `tests/test_hybrid_search.py` (+4 ordering-policy tests), `tests/test_chunk.py` (+1), `docs/evaluation/retrieval/sqlite-vec-2026-09-15.md` (new), the two moved reports, AGENTS.md + CHANGELOG.md.
- **Verification:** `pytest` -> **693 passed, 0 failed**. `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. `eval_search` equivalent, measured through the Gate A harness on 184,171 chunks: before (reference scorer) **0.70 / 0.74 / 0.7297 / 13 failures**, after (sqlite-vec) **0.70 / 0.74 / 0.7297 / 13 failures**, failure sets identical. `sqlite-vec` package **0.1.6** and loaded extension **v0.1.6**, asserted equal by test. Rebuilds ran with the provider pinned; the final one reused 184,105 vectors and computed 66, so nothing was re-embedded by accident and no vector is mock. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) **Phase 1B-FTS** — bound the lexical candidate set, measured alone, under the standing rule that no previously-passing Top-3 query may become a failure and every changed query is named. Semantic KNN only if still needed afterwards; never both in one measurement (the 13h lesson). (2) Native SQL fusion is **deferred**, not rejected — re-profile after 1B-FTS. (3) `scripts/vector_cache.py` is now dead apart from `discard()`, which is kept deliberately as transition hygiene; remove it once the new path clears a real corpus gate. (4) **67 duplicate pages** (625 chunks) remain and are the source of the exact-ties this phase hit — queued as a separately gated corpus cleanup, deliberately NOT bundled here. (5) `wiki/note-test-note-47.md` is a **test artifact living in the live wiki**, contributing 2 chunks to every baseline. (6) The p95 outliers are FTS-dominated (worst 1507 ms on 5 terms / 27,878 rows) and are not explained by row count alone — worth understanding before 1B-FTS picks a bound.

### 2026-09-14d (Australia/Sydney) — Step 0 found a coverage bug, not a ranking problem: the repo's own README, rules and quickstart were never indexed
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** `scripts/chunk.py` (+`ROOT_INDEX_FILES`), `scripts/failure_anatomy.py` (new, Step 0), `scripts/prune_stub_pages.py` (new, P0), `scripts/eval_search.py` (+`failing_queries`), and the two frozen reports under `docs/evaluation/`. Corpus/indexing change, so before/after `eval_search` runs are recorded below per invariant 4.
- **The point of Step 0 was to avoid building the wrong fix, and it worked.** It asked one question before any dependency was installed: could a reranker fix these failures at all? It answered something better — **4 of the 14 failing gold queries expected `README.md`, `AGENTS.md` or `CLAUDE.md`, and all three had ZERO chunks in the index.** The scanner walked `wiki/` and `docs/` only, so Zurvan's own overview, rules and quickstart had never been searchable. Those were **absence failures**: unreachable by reranking, fusion weights, better embeddings, contextual chunks, `sqlite-vec` or KNN, because none of them can rank a document that is not in the index. One missing line of coverage explained **29%** of observed failures, and a storage migration would have made the wrong corpus faster.
- **Classification corrected.** Those four were first labelled `gold_annotation_issue`, which blames the evaluation set for a corpus bug. The gold was right — it named real, important project documents. A new class `index_coverage_miss` carries them; `gold_annotation_issue` survives for a gold entry that is genuinely wrong, and a test asserts the two stay distinct.
- **Phase 0A kept deliberately narrow.** `ROOT_INDEX_FILES = ("README.md", "AGENTS.md", "CLAUDE.md")` — an explicit tuple, never "every root `.md`". Tests assert the three are indexed AND that `SCRATCH_NOTES.md` / `generated-report.md` at the root are not, so a future temporary report or private note cannot be swept in. Further tests re-assert that `raw/` and `wiki/traces/` stay excluded *after* the widening.
- **Coverage was necessary but NOT sufficient — only 1 of 4 recovered.** "rule about pushing to the public remote" now passes; "commands before saying a change is done" is retrieved at rank 5, just outside the top-3; and two queries are now indexed yet still never reach the top-50 at all. That last pair is a chunking/recall problem for Phase 2A, and it was invisible while the documents were absent entirely. Indexing a document does not make it findable.
- **Step 0B, recomputed fresh:** 8/13 rerankable -> `build_2b`. **The rule fired exactly at its boundary** (threshold `>= 8`, measurement 8); one query either way flips it, so it is a pass and not a mandate. No `7/(14-4)` shortcut was taken — that suggestive 7/10 was never measured and was not promoted.
- **`eval_search` now reports `failing_queries`.** It previously reported only a count, and "still 14" cannot prove "the same 14" — the before/after comparison every gate in this project depends on was not previously possible.
- **Files Changed:** `scripts/chunk.py` (+`ROOT_INDEX_FILES`, scanner), `scripts/failure_anatomy.py` (new), `scripts/prune_stub_pages.py` (new), `scripts/eval_search.py` (+`failing_queries`, +`max_queries`), `scripts/ingest.py` (stub generator deleted, `extraction_status`), `tests/test_chunk.py` (+4), `tests/test_failure_anatomy.py` (new, 8), `tests/test_prune_stub_pages.py` (new, 9), `tests/test_eval_search.py` (+5), `tests/test_ingest.py` (+2, 1 obsolete replaced), `docs/evaluation/retrieval-p0-stub-removal-2026-09-14.md` (new), `docs/evaluation/retrieval-step0-failure-analysis-2026-09-14.md` (new), both specs, AGENTS.md + CHANGELOG.md + docs/agents-history.md.
- **Verification:** `pytest` -> **677 passed, 0 failed**. `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. Coverage gate: README 43 / AGENTS 23 / CLAUDE 6 chunks, only those three root files indexed. Safety gate: `raw/` 0 chunks, `wiki/traces/` 0 chunks. **`eval_search --hybrid` before (post-P0, 184,068 chunks) top-1 0.68 / top-3 0.72 / MRR 0.7057 / 14 failures; after (post-0A, 184,157 chunks) top-1 0.70 / top-3 0.74 / MRR 0.7297 / 13 failures, with 0 previously-passing queries broken.** Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Baseline chain (each retirement has a stated reason, none silently overwritten):** pre-P0 199,269 -> post-P0 184,068 -> **post-0A 184,157 · 0.70 / 0.74 / 0.7297 · 13 failures, now CANONICAL**. Phase 1's ranking-invariance gate measures against that row.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) Phase 1 (`sqlite-vec`, full-scan `vec_distance_cosine`, ranking-invariant) is approved and not started. (2) The 5 `candidate_miss` queries belong to Phase 2A — reranking cannot reach them. (3) Phase 2B is justified only at the threshold; re-check the rate after 2A changes what is retrievable. (4) 2 of the 3 newly-indexed root documents are still unretrievable in the top-50 — worth reading why before assuming chunking is the cause.

### 2026-09-14c (Australia/Sydney) — The smoke test was running an evaluation, and the registry was overstating the store
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** The two follow-ups left open by 14b. `scripts/eval_search.py`, `scripts/mcp_tools.py`, `scripts/mcp_server.py`, `scripts/e2e_mcp_smoke.py`, and a new `scripts/prune_registry.py`. No retrieval ranking change — the measurement path is deliberately untouched, which is most of the point of the first fix.
- **1. A smoke test should assert the tool responds, not run an evaluation.** `e2e_mcp_smoke.py` called `zurvan_eval_search` with the full 50-query gold set. At 199k chunks that is ~3 s per query, so the smoke spent minutes measuring retrieval quality it was never asked to measure, and it dominated `check.sh` gate 10. `run_search_evaluation` now takes `max_queries` (plumbed through the MCP tool, and `--max-queries` on the CLI); the smoke uses 2 queries with `min_top3=0.0`, since a threshold calibrated over 50 queries means nothing over 2. **MCP e2e smoke: ~4 minutes -> 36 seconds**, still 0 JSON-RPC parse failures.
- **The guard matters more than the speed.** A sampled run is a liveness check and must never be mistaken for a measurement, so it **labels itself** — `(LIVENESS RUN — first 2 of 50 queries; not a quality measurement)` — and a test asserts that a full run is never labelled that way. `max_queries` defaults to `None`, so `check.sh` gate 6 still evaluates the whole set; that default is also asserted.
- **2. The registry claimed documents the store did not hold.** 115 rows pointed at files no longer on disk. Measuring before fixing changed the fix: of 3,799 rows, **3,650 healthy**, **120 whose source file is gone but whose PAGE survives**, and **29 with no page anywhere**. Only the 29 were false. Pruning the 120 would have been actively harmful — Zurvan still holds that content, and releasing the row would let the same bytes ingest again as a duplicate page, recreating the collision class fixed in 14a. Only a row with no page is a lie, and it is a costly one: the registry refuses a re-ingest, so the content can never come back until the row is released.
- **New `scripts/prune_registry.py`** classifies every row as healthy / held / orphan and is **dry-run by default** — releasing registrations is not something to do by accident. `--apply` released the 29; the registry went **3,799 -> 3,770 rows with 0 orphans**, so every remaining row now corresponds to content that exists. It also covers a state that did not occur today but is worse when it does: a row whose file is PRESENT but whose page is missing — recoverable content that the registry was blocking.
- **Files Changed:** `scripts/eval_search.py` (+`max_queries`, `full_size`, the liveness label, `--max-queries`), `scripts/mcp_tools.py` + `scripts/mcp_server.py` (parameter plumbed through with a schema description saying it is a liveness switch), `scripts/e2e_mcp_smoke.py` (liveness call + the reason, in-line), `scripts/prune_registry.py` (new), `tests/test_eval_search.py` (+4), `tests/test_prune_registry.py` (new, 5), AGENTS.md + CHANGELOG.md + docs/agents-history.md.
- **Verification:** `pytest` -> **644 passed, 0 failed**. `e2e_mcp_smoke.py` -> pass in **36 s** (was ~4 min), 0 `Failed to parse JSONRPC`. `prune_registry.py` re-run after `--apply` reports **0 orphans**. `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. Registry backed up before the prune. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) The **120 held rows** are correct but worth a periodic look — they are documents whose source checkout is gone, so the page in Zurvan is now the only copy. (2) `prune_registry.py` is not wired into `check.sh`; running the report (not `--apply`) as a gate would catch the next drift early. (3) The 67 legacy duplicate pages and 1 orphan page noted in 14a are still there.

*(Older entries: [docs/agents-history.md](docs/agents-history.md))*

### 2026-09-14b (Australia/Sydney) — MCP: bounded results, tool annotations, SDK v2 / protocol 2026-07-28, and a silent stdio corruption
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** The whole MCP surface — `scripts/mcp_server.py`, `mcp_tools.py`, `mcp_resources.py`, `install_mcp_config.py`, `public_repo_guard.py`, plus the client configs and the `zurvan` skill. Audited against the current MCP specification and SDK, then fixed what the audit found. No retrieval ranking change; the `eval_search` run below is the post-corpus baseline re-confirmed through `check.sh`.
- **1. Tool results were unbounded, and the corpus made that dangerous.** `zurvan_graph_neighbours` and `zurvan_graph_expand` had no `limit` and no cursor. Measured on the real 12,050-node graph: the hub node returned **300,055 characters (~75,013 tokens)** from one call, and depth-2 expansion returned **906,501 (~226,625 tokens)** — a single tool result larger than most context windows. Both now page (`limit` default 25, max 200, plus `cursor`), announce `Showing 1-25 of 3659. More available: call again with cursor=25.`, and `graph_expand` orders **nearest-hop first** so a first page is the relevant part of the neighbourhood rather than an arbitrary slice. Every result also passes a 24,000-character budget that states what it dropped. After: **1,119 and 3,100 characters** — 268x and 292x smaller, with nothing made unreachable.
- **2. `zurvan_read_page` refused large files instead of slicing them.** A file over 256 KB returned `Error: File too large` and nothing else, so `wiki/index.md` became the one page an agent could not open. It now takes `offset`/`limit` and returns a slice naming the next offset. A size limit should bound what arrives per call, not make content unreachable.
- **3. No tool carried annotations.** A client could not distinguish a search from a write, so it either over-confirmed everything or under-confirmed the writes. All 13 tools now declare them: `readOnlyHint` on the 9 reads, `destructiveHint: false` on the 4 additive writes (they append a page, never overwrite), `openWorldHint: false` throughout since nothing leaves the machine. They are hints; the load-bearing gate is still `enforce_read_only`, which fails closed on `ZURVAN_MCP_READONLY`.
- **4. New `zurvan_status` tool.** The skill tells agents to check index staleness before trusting search, but that check existed only on the CLI, so an MCP-only agent had no way to know it was querying a stale index — or one holding mock vectors, where ranking is meaningless. It reports staleness, chunk/file counts, the embedding provider (loudly, if mock), graph size and whether writes are enabled. It earned its place immediately by reporting STALE during this session.
- **5. SDK v1 -> v2, protocol revision 2026-07-28.** v1.x is maintenance-only (security fixes). Migrated `FastMCP` -> `MCPServer` (`mcp.server.mcpserver`), pinned `mcp>=2.0.0,<3`. **The v2 API was validated in a throwaway venv before the working environment was touched**, which is how the next item was found rather than shipped.
- **6. The resource template never worked for nested paths — on EITHER version.** `zurvan://file/{path}` is RFC 6570 *simple* expansion, which does not match `/`. Verified on v1.25: `zurvan://file/README.md` resolves, `zurvan://file/wiki/decisions/foo.md` raises `Unknown resource`. Every path in this repo is nested, so the template only ever worked for root-level files. The fix is `{+path}` (reserved expansion), and **v1 cannot register it at all** (`ValueError: Mismatch between URI parameters set() and function parameters {'path'}`) — so the migration is the only way to fix it, which is the concrete justification for upgrading beyond "v1 is maintenance-only". `exempt_params={"path"}` disables the SDK's own traversal check so `/` can pass; `is_safe_path` remains the real and stricter gate, asserted by test.
- **7. `print()` in a tool silently corrupted the protocol, and the smoke test passed anyway.** Under stdio, stdout IS the JSON-RPC channel. `memory.py` prints `Created <path>` on every successful write (9 call sites) and `graph_context` prints `Graph index missing`. The client logged `Failed to parse JSONRPC message from server`, skipped the line, and `e2e_mcp_smoke.py` still printed a pass — success and corruption at the same time. Fixed at the MCP boundary with a `_protocol_safe` decorator on all 14 tool entrypoints (stdout -> stderr, where stdio clients treat it as logging) rather than in the shared library, where the prints are correct for CLI use. One test asserts the property across the **whole** tool surface, so a new tool added without the guard fails the suite.
- **8. `cache_hints`.** The spec's new `CacheableResult`: `ttlMs=300000`, `cacheScope="private"` on the four list methods. Private throughout — these listings describe a personal knowledge base and must never be cached by a shared intermediary.
- **9. The installer generated configs that would break a fresh setup.** `install_mcp_config.py` emitted a bare `python` (PATH-dependent; on this machine `python3` is a Homebrew build with neither `mcp` nor PyYAML), a **relative** `scripts/mcp_server.py` (only resolves if the client launches with the repo as CWD), and `ZURVAN_EMBED_PROVIDER=mock`. The mock default is the dangerous one: queries follow the index so it looks harmless, but any rebuild under that environment overwrites every real vector with a hash — the 0%-recall incident from 13h. All three fixed; the 7 pre-existing installer tests passed before AND after the fix, so 9 were added that actually assert these properties. **`wiki/index/` was also added to `public_repo_guard`'s blocked patterns** — a directory listing of `wiki/sources/` page names leaks private document names even without their text.
- **Files Changed:** `scripts/mcp_server.py` (v2 migration, annotations, `{+path}`, `cache_hints`, `zurvan_status`, paging params), `scripts/mcp_tools.py` (+`_paginate`, `_page_bounds`, `_budget`, `_protocol_safe`, `tool_zurvan_status`; actionable errors), `scripts/mcp_resources.py` (+`read_file_slice`), `scripts/install_mcp_config.py` (+`_interpreter`, `_server_args`, `_env`), `scripts/public_repo_guard.py` (+`wiki/index/`), `requirements.txt` + `requirements-dev.txt` (`mcp>=2.0.0,<3`), `tests/test_mcp_tools.py` (+9), `tests/test_mcp_server_smoke.py` (+4), `tests/test_install_mcp_config.py` (+9), AGENTS.md + CHANGELOG.md + docs/agents-history.md.
- **Verification:** `pytest` -> **635 passed, 0 failed**. **`check.sh` -> all 23 gates passed (exit 0)**, including the MCP E2E and the retrieval gate. `e2e_mcp_smoke.py` -> pass with **0 `Failed to parse JSONRPC` and 0 ValidationError** (was 1 before the stdout guard). `doctor_mcp.py` healthy. `public_repo_guard` passed; `git diff --check` clean. `eval_search --hybrid` -> top-1 68% / top-3 72% / MRR 0.71, unchanged by this work. Both client configs verified by launching the server exactly as configured: SDK **2.2.0**, 13 tools, nested resource reads OK, traversal and `raw/` still blocked. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) **`e2e_mcp_smoke.py` calls `zurvan_eval_search`**, which now runs the full 50-query eval against 199k chunks — it dominates the smoke's runtime and made `check.sh` gate 10 take minutes. The smoke should assert the tool responds, not run a full evaluation. (2) The **`SessionStart` hook in `~/.claude/settings.json` was rebuilding the index with `mock` embeddings** (`get_embedding` defaults to mock, and the hook runs in other repos where Zurvan's settings do not apply) inside a 120s timeout — a second, unfixed path to the 13h poisoning plus a truncation risk. Fixed outside this repo by pinning the provider and dropping `--fix-stale`; **anything that can start a rebuild must pin the provider**. (3) Deprecated in 2026-07-28 and still to be reviewed: Roots, Sampling, Logging. (4) `server/discover`, MRTR/`resultType` and the Tasks extension are handled by the SDK, not exercised by Zurvan's own code — worth a look if the server ever grows long-running work.

### 2026-09-14 (Australia/Sydney) — The research archive went in, and five defects only a big corpus could expose came out
**Raouf:**
- **Scope:** Bulk ingest of the Anthropic/OpenAI/DeepMind research archive (`~/Desktop/Resaerch`) into Zurvan, plus the five defects that ingest surfaced in `scripts/ingest.py`, `scripts/chunk.py`, `scripts/rebuild_search_index.py`, `scripts/hybrid_search.py` and the wiki index. Corpus/indexing change, so the before/after `eval_search` is recorded below per invariant 4.
- **The ingest.** 3,037 archive files (PDF + Markdown). 2,996 registered, 40 refused as byte-identical duplicates of documents already held, 1 skipped. Store went 861 -> 3,823 source pages; index 43,528 -> 199,110 chunks; graph 2,786/1,624 -> 12,050/11,262 nodes/edges.
- **1. `create_source_page` silently overwrote on slug collision — the worst of the five.** It wrote `wiki/sources/<slug>.md` with a bare `open(..., 'w')`. Sanitised slugs are NOT unique: **24 different `README.md` files shared one page**, and 484 archive documents lost their text to an identically-titled twin (a paper's PDF clobbered by its web-article version). Measured: 462 colliding slugs over 972 entries, **510 shadowed**. The registry went on claiming every one of them, so the store asserted it held a document whose text on disk belonged to another — and re-ingesting the loser was refused as "already ingested". Nothing anywhere said the content was gone. Fixed with a `-2`/`-3` suffix loop keyed on the frontmatter hash (so a re-ingest reuses its own page rather than growing a suffixevery run), exactly the loop `ingest_image_stub` had always had; `create_stub_concept_and_claim` now takes the resolved page name so the stubs link to the right page instead of reintroducing the clobbering one level down. **578 documents re-ingested** to recover.
- **2. A full rebuild was OOM-killed.** Three sinks all scaled with corpus size: `_harvest_reusable_embeddings` loaded every stored vector into a dict (>1 GB of JSON strings before the first write), `chunk_all_markdown()` materialised all chunks in one list, and the whole run was one transaction — so the kill left 1.7 GB of uncommitted garbage and zero progress. Now: streaming `iter_all_markdown_chunks()` (the list wrapper stays for existing callers), a `_PriorIndex` class that moves the old index to `.prev` and looks up each vector **on disk** by primary key, and `COMMIT_EVERY = 2000`. Rebuild peak RSS is flat at ~455 MB.
- **3. The test suite stopped finishing.** `tests/test_hybrid_search.py` built its index from the LIVE wiki. At 861 pages that was seconds; at 12,047 markdown files it ran over two hours without completing. A unit test's runtime must depend on its fixture, never on how much a developer has ingested. `rebuild_search_index` gained `root=`, and the tests build a three-file corpus in `tmp_path`.
- **4. Search marshalled the entire corpus on every query.** `search_hybrid` ran `SELECT c.text, e.vector FROM chunks JOIN embeddings` with `fetchall()` and a pure-Python cosine per row — ~2 GB per search at 199k chunks, measured at **31-50 s**. Rewritten to stream `(chunk_id, vector)` in batches through a numpy matrix-vector product reduced by a bounded heap, hydrating text only for the survivors. The remaining cost was `json.loads` on 199k vectors per query, so `scripts/vector_cache.py` adds a float32 `.vectors.npy` sidecar (rebuilt whenever its count disagrees with the index, so a stale pair can never be used) that moves the parse to the write path. **31-50 s -> 3.0 s mean**, peak RSS 894 MB. Ranking is asserted identical to the reference cosine+fusion by test; scores agree to float32 resolution (~1e-9), and the ORDER is the contract.
- **5. `wiki/index.md` had become unreadable.** `update_index` appended a line per ingest with no de-duplication and no bound: **501 KB over 3,713 lines, 450 of them duplicates**, past the 256 KB MCP read cap — so `zurvan_read_page wiki/index.md` returned "File too large" and the index was the one page an agent could not open. New `scripts/wiki_index.py` keeps `index.md` as a table of contents (**1.1 KB**, 27 shards, 3,822 pages) with entries in `wiki/index/sources-<shard>.md` (largest 98 KB) and idempotent `add_entry`. **`wiki/index/` is gitignored AND added to `public_repo_guard`'s blocked patterns** — a directory listing of `wiki/sources/` page names leaks private document names even without their text.
- **Audit.** Full chain re-verified after the fixes: 2,996/2,996 registry rows have their own hash-matched page; 0 empty, 0 under 200 chars, 0 missing frontmatter; 2,996/2,996 present in the search index; 0 PDF-derived ligature defects across 979 PDFs (the 7 archive pages carrying ligature glyphs all have `.md` sources, where the glyph is the author's own text and correctly passed through). The 1 un-ingested file is `Decoupled Neural Interfaces Using Synthetic Gradients.pdf`, whose page tree is damaged (8 of 29 pages unreachable) — its content is in the store twice under other names, so nothing is missing. 67 redundant pages share a content hash, **0 of them archive-derived** (44 are legacy `.md.md` double-suffix names predating the extension-stripping change); they contribute 625 chunks, 0.31% of the index, and were left in place.
- **Files Changed:** `scripts/ingest.py` (+`read_page_hash`, `resolve_source_page_path`; `create_source_page`/`create_stub_concept_and_claim`/`update_index`), `scripts/chunk.py` (+`iter_all_markdown_chunks`), `scripts/rebuild_search_index.py` (+`_PriorIndex`, `COMMIT_EVERY`, `root=`, cache warm/discard), `scripts/hybrid_search.py` (+`_score_semantic_top_k`, `_hydrate`, `_vector_batches`, `_cursor_db_path`), `scripts/vector_cache.py` (new), `scripts/wiki_index.py` (new), `scripts/public_repo_guard.py` (+`wiki/index/`), `.gitignore` (+`data/*.vectors.*`, `wiki/index/`), `tests/test_ingest.py` (+3), `tests/test_hybrid_search.py` (fixture corpus + equivalence test), `tests/test_wiki_index.py` (new, 4), AGENTS.md + CHANGELOG.md + docs/agents-history.md.
- **Verification:** `pytest` -> **611 passed, 0 failed** (509 s). `public_repo_guard` passed. `git diff --check` clean. **Frozen provenance golds unchanged** (86% / 100% / 0% / 100% / 100%). `eval_search --hybrid` before (43,528 chunks) **top-1 70% / top-3 72% / MRR 0.72 / 14 failures**; after (199,110 chunks) **top-1 68% / top-3 72% / MRR 0.71 / 14 failures** — exactly one query moved from rank 1 to rank 3 and the failure set is unchanged, so a 4.6x corpus increase did not dilute ranking. Committed locally; **not pushed**.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) **115 registry rows point at files no longer on disk** (deleted local checkouts); 29 of those were also clobbered, so their text is unrecoverable — the store overstates what it holds until they are pruned or the sources re-obtained. (2) The non-hybrid `eval_search` path is a naive whole-file substring count and now reports **0%** at this corpus size — it is not the documented metric (hybrid is) but it is misleading enough to either fix or delete. (3) The 67 legacy duplicate pages and 1 orphan page are pre-existing cruft worth a cleanup pass. (4) The archive's own audit calls every PDF valid because it checks `%%EOF`; that misses a broken page tree, as the DeepMind paper shows. (5) A 2 GB index and 306 MB sidecar are now the working set — worth revisiting if the store grows again.

### 2026-09-13i (Australia/Sydney) — The privacy guard was publishing the private names it exists to protect
**Raouf:**
